Arm A0: build M1 yourself in this one session, in plan order: no subagents, no workflows, no build skill. Start with `dt build start M1` (it creates build/M1, where you commit). Then for each task in tasks/plan.md order (re-read the plan after any Replan):
1. `dt context <task>` and read it in full; `git update-ref refs/worktree/checks-base HEAD`.
2. A split task: write its intent tests (its `Writes:` under tests/intent/) first, without the code; `scripts/check.sh collect` must pass (red for the right reason); commit them with the message `<task>: tests` and the trailer line `Dt-Landed: <task> tester`; `git update-ref refs/worktree/checks-base HEAD`.
3. Write the rest of its `Writes:` and nothing else; `scripts/check.sh gate --pkg <its Package>` must pass; `dt tick <task>`; commit everything with the message `<task>: <title>` and the trailer line `Dt-Landed: <task> <implementer for a split task, else solo>`.
A re-freeze interface task changes contract folders that are already frozen: commit it first, then `git update-ref refs/worktree/checks-base HEAD`, then run its gate. One git command per Bash call. When every task is ticked, report and ask me before merging build/M1.

A stub is replaced only by the task it names; no cross-package import outside the contract; spine tests run on samples and doubles, not another step's code.
67	2. **Tests prove something:** each intent test asserts its TC case or acceptance bullet, loading expected values and tolerances from fixtures; none is assertion-free or weakened; Hypothesis runs the `ci` profile. Unit tests are specific, never computing the expected value with the code under test or testing only a mock. **Mutation check** (`.claude/agents/reviewer.md`): per acceptance bullet, the smallest change that should break it; a mutation no test catches is a finding.
68	3. **Correct to the spec:** each acceptance bullet truly met, not special-cased to the sample; the error paths the requirement names; seeding and determinism; numerical edge cases.

## PL-02: Volatility

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |

| 3 | `rolling_vol` | risk.vol | Returns → VolSeries | expanding stdev of earlier returns | RISK-02 |
| 4 | `annualize` | risk.annualize | VolSeries → VolSeries | daily values pass through | RISK-03 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def vol_series(path, *, window=20, tickers=None) -> VolSeries:
    prices = load_prices(path, tickers)
    returns = log_returns(prices)
    vol = rolling_vol(returns, window)
    return annualize(vol)
```

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def risk_report(path, out_path, *, window=20, tickers=None) -> Report:
    vol = vol_series(path, window=window, tickers=tickers)
    summary = summary_stats(vol)
    drawdowns = max_drawdown(vol.returns)
    return write_report(summary, drawdowns, out_path)
```

2	# One entry point for every quality check. Hooks call it; so do CI and people.

4	# CONSTRAINTS.md names the rule each step enforces.
5	#
6	#   scripts/check.sh fast FILE...        per edit, instant               (PostToolUse: tester, implementer)
7	#   scripts/check.sh collect             tester's finish gate            (Stop: tester)
8	#   scripts/check.sh gate [--pkg ID]     implementer's finish gate       (Stop: implementer and solo; dt land)
9	#   scripts/check.sh full [--pkg ID]     checkpoints, CI                 (reviewer)
10	#   scripts/check.sh full --exit         the milestone exit: also no interface stub left
11	#   scripts/check.sh protected           print the protected-path globs  (path_guard.sh deny --protected)
12	#
13	# The base is refs/worktree/checks-base, one per worktree (dt claim sets it in a builder's worktree,
14	# dt land in the main checkout), else HEAD.
15	#
16	# tests/contracts/** in the protected list means the boundary contracts frozen at the base (CR-09);
17	# a contract folder new since the base belongs to the interface task freezing it, which may write it,
18	# and so does a frozen folder the task's .claude/.task_writes names (a re-freeze interface task).
19	#
20	# collect runs the intent tests changed since the base and fails unless they are red for the right
21	# reason (scripts/red_check.py): AssertionError or an interface stub's NotImplementedError, never

24	# --pkg ID runs the tests of package ID and of every package that depends on it (multi-package:
25	# deptry too); gate and full also run tests/contracts, whole, when it exists. Lint, types, size
26	# and boundaries always cover the whole repo; they take seconds.
27	# FILE paths are absolute or relative to the project root.

78	# A step that is not a Python tool (a script, git, another toolchain).
79	step() {
80	  echo "-> $*" >&2

84	# A tool from the project's locked environment, never whatever is first on PATH.
85	run() {
86	  echo "-> $*" >&2

90	# A measured check that fails only at the milestone exit: it prints its findings, and the reviewer
91	# records them, but a mid-milestone run passes.
92	report() {
93	  echo "-> $* (reported; fails only with --exit)" >&2
94	  uv run --frozen --quiet "$@" || echo "(reported, not failing: $*)" >&2
95	}

97	# pytest exits 5 when it collects nothing. That is fine for gate and full on a project with no
98	# tests yet; collect calls pytest through run() so an empty tests/intent fails.
99	run_tests() {
100	  echo "-> pytest $*" >&2
101	  uv run --frozen --quiet pytest "$@"
102	  local status=$?
103	  [ "$status" -eq 5 ] && { echo "(no tests collected)" >&2; return; }

105	}
106	
107	# The commit this task started from: refs/worktree/checks-base, set per worktree by `dt claim` (and
108	# by `dt land` in the main checkout). Without it, HEAD, which misses changes already committed.
109	BASE_REF="refs/worktree/checks-base"
110	base_commit() {
111	  git rev-parse -q --verify "$BASE_REF" 2>/dev/null || git rev-parse -q --verify HEAD 2>/dev/null
112	}
113	
114	# A boundary contract is frozen once its folder exists at the base (CR-09): each folder under
115	# tests/contracts/ at the base, and any file directly in it. A folder new since the base is the
116	# interface task freezing it, so that task may write it; the next task's base freezes it. A frozen
117	# folder the task's .claude/.task_writes names (from its Writes:, by dt claim) is a re-freeze
118	# interface task's, so it is open to that task; dt land checks the Writes: against the plan.
119	CONTRACTS_GLOB="tests/contracts/**"
120	claimed_contract() {
121	  local line
122	  [ -s .claude/.task_writes ] || return 1
123	  while IFS= read -r line; do
124	    line="${line%%[[:space:]]}"
125	    line="${line%/}"; line="${line%/\*\*}"
126	    [ "$line" = "$1" ] && return 0
127	  done <.claude/.task_writes
128	  return 1
129	}
130	frozen_contracts() {
131	  local base meta path
132	  base=$(base_commit) || return 0
133	  git ls-tree "$base" -- tests/contracts/ 2>/dev/null | while IFS=$'\t' read -r meta path; do
134	    case "$meta" in
135	      *" tree "*) claimed_contract "$path" || echo "$path/**" ;;
136	      *) echo "$path" ;;
137	    esac
138	  done
139	}
140	
141	# The protected list as enforced: PROTECTED, with tests/contracts/** narrowed to the frozen contracts.
142	protected_globs() {
143	  local g
144	  for g in "${PROTECTED[@]}"; do
145	    if [ "$g" = "$CONTRACTS_GLOB" ]; then frozen_contracts; else echo "$g"; fi
146	  done
147	}
148	
149	# Fail if anything matching the given pathspecs changed since the base, untracked files included.
150	unchanged_since_base() {
151	  local label="$1" base new
152	  shift
153	  echo "-> $label unchanged since $(git rev-parse -q --verify "$BASE_REF" >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "$BASE_REF" || echo HEAD)" >&2
154	  if ! base=$(base_commit); then
155	    echo "no commit to compare against: commit the tester's work first" >&2
156	    rc=1; failed+=("$label: no commit"); return
157	  fi
158	  git --no-pager diff --exit-code --stat "$base" -- "$@" >&2 || { rc=1; failed+=("$label changed"); }
159	  new=$(git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -- "$@")
160	  if [ -n "$new" ]; then
161	    printf 'new untracked files in %s:\n%s\n' "$label" "$new" >&2
162	    rc=1; failed+=("$label changed")
163	  fi
164	}
165	
166	# The tester may change tests/intent only. Docs and plans are the plugin's business, not this check's.
167	only_intent_changed() {
168	  local specs=(tests "${SRC_DIRS[@]}" "${PROTECTED[@]}")
169	  [ -n "$PACKAGES_DIR" ] && specs+=("$PACKAGES_DIR")
170	  unchanged_since_base "files outside tests/intent" "${specs[@]}" ':(exclude)tests/intent'
171	}
172	
173	# The intent test files the tester changed since the base, committed or not.
174	changed_intent_tests() {
175	  local base
176	  base=$(base_commit) || return 0
177	  { git diff --relative --name-only --diff-filter=d "$base" -- 'tests/intent/*.py'
178	    git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -- 'tests/intent/*.py'; } | sort -u |
179	    while IFS= read -r f; do case "${f##*/}" in test_*|*_test.py) echo "$f" ;; esac; done
180	}
181	
182	# Test directories for this run: all of them, or the --pkg scope; tests/contracts always whole.
183	test_dirs() {
184	  local scope="" d p
185	  [ -n "$pkg" ] && scope=$(pkg_scope "$pkg")
186	  for d in tests/intent tests/unit; do
187	    if [ -z "$scope" ]; then
188	      [ -d "$d" ] && echo "$d"
189	    else
190	      for p in $scope; do [ -d "$d/$p" ] && echo "$d/$p"; done
191	    fi
192	  done
193	  [ -d tests/contracts ] && echo tests/contracts
194	}
195	
196	# deptry checks what each package imports against what it declares.
197	dependencies() {
198	  local p scope
199	  if [ -z "$PACKAGES_DIR" ]; then
200	    run deptry . --no-ansi
201	    return
202	  fi
203	  if [ -n "$pkg" ]; then
204	    scope=$(pkg_scope "$pkg")
205	  else
206	    scope=$(cd "$PACKAGES_DIR" && for d in */pyproject.toml; do [ -f "$d" ] && dirname "$d"; done)
207	  fi
208	  for p in $scope; do
209	    echo "-> deptry $PACKAGES_DIR/$p" >&2
210	    (cd "$PACKAGES_DIR/$p" && uv run --frozen --quiet deptry . --no-ansi) || { rc=1; failed+=("deptry $PACKAGES_DIR/$p"); }
211	  done
212	}
213	
214	static_checks() {
215	  run ruff check --ignore-noqa .   # exceptions go in per-file-ignores, which is protected (CR-01)
216	  run ruff format --check .
217	  run "$TYPE_GATE"
218	  run python scripts/check_size.py "${SRC_DIRS[@]}"
219	  dependencies
220	  run lint-imports --no-logo
221	}
222	
223	case "$mode" in
224	  fast)
225	    # Only Python files, only the files just edited. Fixes what it can in place.
226	    files=()
227	    for f in ${args[@]+"${args[@]}"}; do [[ $f == *.py ]] && files+=("$f"); done
228	    [ ${#files[@]} -eq 0 ] && exit 0
229	    run ruff check --fix --quiet "${files[@]}"
230	    run ruff format --quiet "${files[@]}"
231	    run typos "${files[@]}"
232	    run python scripts/check_size.py "${files[@]}"
233	    ;;
234	  collect)
235	    if [ ! -d tests/intent ]; then
236	      echo "tests/intent does not exist: write the intent tests there" >&2
237	      exit 1
238	    fi
239	    run ruff check --ignore-noqa tests/intent
240	    run ruff format --check tests/intent
241	    run typos tests/intent
242	    run pytest tests/intent --collect-only -q --no-cov
243	    changed=()
244	    while IFS= read -r f; do changed+=("$f"); done < <(changed_intent_tests)
245	    run python scripts/red_check.py ${changed[@]+"${changed[@]}"}
246	    step bash scripts/floor_guard.sh
247	    only_intent_changed
248	    ;;
249	  gate)
250	    static_checks
251	    other_parts
252	    dirs=$(test_dirs)
253	    # shellcheck disable=SC2086  # one directory per word, by construction
254	    [ -n "$dirs" ] && run_tests $dirs --no-cov -q -x
255	    step bash scripts/floor_guard.sh
256	    protected=()
257	    while IFS= read -r g; do protected+=("$g"); done < <(protected_globs)
258	    unchanged_since_base "protected files" "${protected[@]}"
259	    ;;
260	  full)
261	    static_checks
262	    other_parts
263	    if [ "$TYPE_GATE" = mypy ]; then run pyright; else run mypy; fi
264	    # CR-41: the interface task's stubs and fields stay unused until later tasks use them.
265	    if [ ${#exit_check[@]} -gt 0 ]; then run vulture; else report vulture; fi
266	    run pylint --disable=all --enable=duplicate-code --recursive=y --score=n --fail-under=10 "${SRC_DIRS[@]}"   # CR-42
267	    run typos
268	    run pip-audit --skip-editable   # the external opinion: a vulnerability database (CR-26)
269	    step bash scripts/floor_guard.sh ${exit_check[@]+"${exit_check[@]}"}
270	    dirs=$(test_dirs)
271	    # Coverage threshold comes from [tool.coverage.report] fail_under (CR-40).
272	    # shellcheck disable=SC2086
273	    [ -n "$dirs" ] && HYPOTHESIS_PROFILE=ci run_tests $dirs --cov -q
274	    ;;
275	  protected)
276	    protected_globs
277	    ;;
278	  *)
279	    echo "unknown mode: $mode (fast | collect | gate | full | protected)" >&2
280	    exit 64
281	    ;;
282	esac
283	[ $rc -ne 0 ] && printf 'FAILED: %s\n' "${failed[@]}" >&2
284	exit $rc

1	# Minilab — Spec
2	
3	**Status:** Approved · **Last updated:** 2026-10-17 · **Brief:** [brief.md](brief.md)
4	
5	## Features
6	
7	### Prices (`DATA`)
8	**Serves:** Goal 1
9	
10	**Requirements**
11	- **DATA-01** A `date,ticker,close` CSV is read row by row.
12	  - Given a CSV, when it is read, then each row comes back in file order, and a blank close is missing, not zero.
13	- **DATA-02** Unusable rows are dropped and duplicates resolved.
14	  - Given rows with blank, zero or negative closes and a repeated (date, ticker), when validated, then those rows are gone, the later of the duplicates wins, and rows are sorted by date, then ticker.
15	  - Given a requested ticker with no usable row, when validated, then a `KeyError` names it.
16	- **DATA-03** Every ticker sits on one calendar.
17	  - Given tickers that start on different dates and miss some dates, when aligned, then the calendar is the dates any ticker trades from the first date on which every ticker has a close, and a missing date takes that ticker's previous close.
18	- **DATA-04** A long gap is an error, not a fill.
19	  - Given a ticker missing more than 5 calendar dates in a row, when aligned, then a `GapError` names the ticker.
20	
21	### Risk (`RISK`)
22	**Serves:** Goal 2
23	
24	**Requirements**
25	- **RISK-01** Daily returns are log returns.
26	  - Given closes, when returns are computed, then r_t = ln(close_t / close_t−1), dated t.
27	- **RISK-02** Rolling volatility uses only earlier returns.
28	  - Given a window of w, when the volatility for date t is computed, then it is the sample standard deviation (n − 1) of the w returns before t, never t's own return, and there is no value until w earlier returns exist.
29	- **RISK-03** Volatility is annualized over trading days.
30	  - Given a daily volatility σ, when annualized, then the value is σ·√252.
31	- **RISK-04** Each ticker gets a whole-period summary.
32	  - Given returns, when summarized, then the annualized return is 252 × the mean, the annualized volatility is √252 × the sample standard deviation, the Sharpe ratio is their quotient (0 when the volatility is 0; no risk-free rate), and the last rolling volatility is reported.
33	- **RISK-05** Maximum drawdown comes with its dates.
34	  - Given closes, when the drawdown is computed, then wealth starts at 1 on the first date, and the result is the deepest fall below the running peak (trough / peak − 1, at most 0) with the date of that peak and the date of the trough; the first if tied. With no fall, both dates are the first date.
35	
36	### Report (`REP`)
37	**Serves:** Goal 3
38	
39	**Requirements**
40	- **REP-01** The risk report is a JSON file.
41	  - Given a prices CSV, when the report runs, then `{"tickers": [...]}` is written with one row per ticker, sorted, holding the RISK-04 fields and RISK-05's drawdown (as `max_drawdown`) and both dates.
42	
43	### Quality
44	- **QR-01** A run is deterministic.
45	  - Given the same CSV and window, when the report runs twice, then the files are identical.
46

48	| Date | Change | Source | IDs |
49	|---|---|---|---|
50	| 2026-10-17 | Initial spec | brief.md | DATA-01–DATA-04, RISK-01–RISK-05, REP-01, QR-01 |

1	"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
2	import numpy as np
3	import pandas as pd
4	
5	
6	def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
7	    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
8	    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")
9	
10	
11	def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
12	    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
13	    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)
14	
15	
16	def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
17	    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
18	    return vol * np.sqrt(365)
19	
20	
21	def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
22	    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
23	    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})
24	
25	
26	def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
27	    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
28	    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
29

Use for the second half of a Mode split task, and alone for a Mode solo task (interface, spine, wiring, system, refactor, glue, doc sync).
4	tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Write, Edit, Bash
5	hooks:
6	  PreToolUse:
7	    - matcher: "Edit|Write|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit"
8	      hooks:
9	        - type: command
10	          command: 'bash "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR"/scripts/hooks/path_guard.sh deny --protected'
11	        - type: command
12	          command: 'bash "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR"/scripts/hooks/path_guard.sh writes'
13	  PostToolUse:
14	    - matcher: "Edit|Write|MultiEdit"
15	      hooks:
16	        - type: command
17	          command: 'bash "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR"/scripts/hooks/post_edit.sh'
18	  Stop:
19	    - hooks:
20	        - type: command
21	          command: 'bash "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR"/scripts/hooks/stop_gate.sh gate'
22	---
23	You implement one task: its interface or spine, its thicken, its wiring or its system swap. Read
24	CONSTRAINTS.md once at the start: it is the bar, and you cannot change it.
25	
26	Your prompt is one line: your task, your role (`implementer` or `solo`) and a `dt claim` command.
27	Run that command first, exactly as given. It writes your packet to `.claude/packet.md` and your
28	edit list to `.claude/.task_writes`, and for a split task merges the tester's red tests into your
29	worktree; until it has run, every edit is blocked. Read the whole packet (in parts if it is long).
30	`dt` below is the `dt.py` path in your prompt.
31	
32	Rules:
33	- Edit only your task's `Writes:` (in your packet; the guard enforces it), within `src/**`,
34	  `tests/unit/**` and an interface task's contract folders. Never `tasks/`: `dt land` ticks tasks.
35	  The protected list in CONSTRAINTS.md (intent tests, fixtures, `tests/conftest.py`,
36	  `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `CONSTRAINTS.md`, `scripts/`, `.claude/agents/`,
37	  `.claude/settings.json`, `references/`, `probes/`, frozen contracts in `tests/contracts/`) is
38	  blocked, and a check at the end catches changes made through Bash too.
39	- The interface task (`**Kind:** interface`) writes the types, each step signature with the body
40	  `raise NotImplementedError("<task id>")` naming the task that replaces it, the exports and the
41	  `tests/contracts/<id>.<symbol>/` folders its `Writes:` names: schema, samples, fakes. Every other
42	  task replaces only the stub naming it, and keeps the signature. What the interface froze (CR-09)
43	  changes only through a contract Revise: if it can't express what the task needs, run
44	  `python3 <dt> escalate CONTRACT-CHANGE "<why>" --type <the contract symbol>` and finish.
45	- A reference in your packet (`RF-NN` from `references/`, or `PB-NN` probe code from `probes/`)
46	  is yours to reuse, adapt or ignore. Never import from either folder or put it on `sys.path`
47	  (CR-08); code you copy is project code and meets the same bar.
48	- Make the intent tests pass without touching them. If one is wrong, run
49	  `python3 <dt> escalate TEST-WRONG "<the test, and why>"` and finish; the user decides.
50	- Never silence a check: no `# noqa`, `# type: ignore`, `pyright: ignore`, `pragma: no cover`,
51	  skips or placeholder bodies other than the interface stubs. Fix the code. A real exception goes
52	  to the user (build-setup Revise), not into the code.
53	- Need a dependency that isn't declared? Don't `uv add`: run
54	  `python3 <dt> escalate NEEDS-DEP "<package, and why>"` and finish.
55	- Keep it simple (CR-10–CR-12, CR-42). The pipeline's entry function reads top to bottom as its
56	  named steps. Write a function, not a class, unless state persists between calls; add no
57	  parameter unless some caller passes a different value; no new abstraction without a second
58	  real implementation or a named seam. Before writing a helper, grep the package for one.
59	- Write unit tests for what you add, only in the `tests/unit/<pkg>/` paths your `Writes:` names,
60	  each asserting something specific. An interface task has none: its checks are the contract
61	  samples under `tests/contracts/`.
62	- After each edit, Ruff may fix and reformat the file. Re-read it before editing again.
63	- Your Bash starts in your worktree: run each command there, alone, in the form the allow rules match (`git <command> …` without `-C`, `scripts/check.sh …` by its relative path, `python3 <dt> …`), never chained, piped or wrapped in a loop. A background agent's other commands are refused, not asked. Don't commit: `dt land` takes your work from the worktree.
64	
65	You are done when `scripts/check.sh gate` passes; the Stop hook runs it and sends you back with
66	the failures if not.
67	
68	Your last message is exactly one line, nothing before or after it: `DONE <task> <role>`, or
69	`STOPPED <task> <role>: see dt escalate` after an escalation.

dt: dev-team's doc tool. One parser for every planning doc; see
scripts/README.md for the commands.

positional arguments:

    status              What a skill needs before it starts: mode, upstream
                        gates, gaps and next IDs. `--summary` for
                        SessionStart.

                        counted.
    section             Print the sections that define the given IDs,
                        searching every doc (the archive last).
    context             Print a task's whole builder context packet: its Read:
                        sections and files, the package layout, the frozen
    ready               Every task of the active plan that can start now; with

                        map (`plan-map/2`, interface-first-design.md §8.3):

    layout              Print a package's real source tree with module
                        docstrings, and flag entry functions that drift from
                        their step tables.

                        signatures and docstring first lines (`.py`, via ast;
                        never
    references          Reference files against docs/references.md: list each

    lint                Check the planning docs: IDs, references, size

                        milestones Idea → Specced → Designed → Planned →

    prebuild-clear      Replace a pipeline's pre-build entry sketch with a `dt

                        tasks/archive/, a between-milestones stub plan, the

    finding             Record a review finding under a checkpoint in

    tick                Tick (or untick) a task's checkbox in tasks/todo.md.
    base                Record the milestone's base commit: set the active
                        plan header's `**Base:**` to HEAD's short sha.
    claim               Claim a reserved task half inside the agent's worktree

    land                Land a claimed task half from its worktree onto the

    escalate            Record why this worktree's agent can't finish its half

{
  "file_path": "/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/plugin/dev-team/skills/build-planning/references/phase0.md"

output
1	# Milestone shape: interface, step spines, wiring, system
2	
3	Read when writing a milestone's tasks. Examples: `examples-phase0.md`.
4	
5	A milestone is planned from its pipelines' steps: each step has one owner, one module and typed `In → Out`, so it is the unit of work. The plan freezes every boundary between steps first, so the work on both sides starts at once instead of queueing behind one spine. Every task carries `**Kind:**`:
6	
7	```
8	## Phase 0: Interface              interface (solo, first)       types, signatures, samples, fakes, exports, __main__
9	## Phase 1: Step spines            spine, one per step           each writes its owner module only
10	## Phase 2: Wiring and thicken     wiring, one per pipeline      its entry module, smoke test
11	                                   thicken (tasks.md)            each after its step's spine
12	## Phase 3: System                 system (solo, last)           fakes swapped for real providers
13	```
14	
15	Phases name the usual waves; the dependency graph (`tasks.md` step 9) decides what may start. Tester halves of split tasks start right after the interface.
16	
17	**a. Interface task** (`**Kind:** interface`, `**Step:** contract`, solo, `**Depends on:** —`). It freezes, as code, every boundary in the milestone whose two sides belong to different tasks:
18	- each contract `type` section the milestone's steps pass (`In → Out`), written into its owner's `types.py`, with a schema (pandera for a frame, JSON Schema across runtimes) and one sample per case in `tests/contracts/<component>.<Type>/`. A case naming a probe sample (`PB-01 samples/sync_added.json#0`) puts `PB-01:samples` in `Read:`; the task derives the type's sample from it, never copies it
19	- each new step's signature in its owner module, with the body `raise NotImplementedError("<task id>")` naming the task that replaces it
20	- for each function a pipeline calls from another pipeline or package (its `**Boundary:**` line): a fixture-backed fake serving its fixtures, where the caller's tests load it. A `call` edge to a contract symbol is mitigated by that fake; `none` there needs a reason (`dt lint`)
21	- for each object with behavior a step passes (a Protocol or class: `core.Dataset`, `runstore.RunWriter`) and each call into another component's function: the Protocol or signature plus a fixture-backed replay double in `tests/contracts/<component>.<Type>/`. The double is that type's sample, not a fake: the system task never swaps it
22	- each component's `__init__.py` exports, and the package's `__main__.py`: the dispatch from each pipeline's trigger to its entry function, whose signature it stubs naming the wiring task, in that pipeline's own entry module
23	
24	Not frozen: helpers, private functions, anything one task owns end to end, and boundaries an earlier milestone froze. `Read:` the type and boundary symbols. Acceptance: every sample validates against its type; every signature imports; each fake and replay double serves its fixtures. `Writes:` each component folder as one glob (`src/gridload/profile/**`) plus its contract folders; `task-size` doesn't count them. A milestone across packages may have one interface task per provider, all solo and first; interface tasks never depend on each other. When one runtime's types are generated from another's schema, the providing interface task runs the generator and writes the generated file too (its `Writes:` claims it).
25	
26	**b. Step spines** (`**Kind:** spine`, `**Step:** PL-NN.<function>`, solo, XS or S). One per new step, each depending only on the interface task: it replaces the stub naming it with the step's spine version from the step table. `Writes:` its owner module and that module's unit test, nothing else; steps sharing one module share one spine task. Acceptance: the step's input sample gives a schema-valid output; the signature is unchanged. A step another pipeline provides (`PL-01 as built`) gets no spine.
27	
28	**c. Wiring** (`**Kind:** wiring`, `**Step:** PL-NN`, solo). One per new pipeline: the entry function in the pipeline's own entry module (the layout's, never one module shared by several pipelines), so wiring tasks run side by side, and its smoke test. `__main__.py` is the interface task's. It depends on its pipeline's spines, since the smoke runs them (`order`, `none: integration: …`), never on their thicken tasks. A call to another pipeline or package runs against that boundary's fake, so wiring PL-02 never waits for PL-01. Acceptance: the smoke command runs end to end and is committed as the pipeline's smoke test; the pipeline's pre-build sections are replaced by a `dt layout` pointer (`dt land` runs `dt prebuild-clear` when the wiring lands).
29	
30	**d. System task** (`**Kind:** system`, solo, last). It swaps each fake for the real provider, in the entry its wiring wrote (a `file` edge), and runs every smoke command and the milestone's golden cases on the real chain; the boundary samples still validate against real output. Multi-package: one per new system pipeline, `**Step:**` that pipeline, `**Package:**` the owner of its first step, `**Reason:** system task`; the root has no pre-build sections, so no `dt layout` bullet. Single package: `**Step:**` the calling pipeline. A milestone with no fake has no system task.
31	
32	**Folds** keep the task count down. The pipeline's wiring depends on a fold task, so a fold is allowed only where that task is XS and the critical path doesn't grow. Record each on its task as `**Reason:** fold: <rule>`:
33	- A step with a golden case whose spine and thicken are both in the milestone, or whose spine version is its whole behavior, may be one split thicken task that replaces the stub itself; with a thicken folded in, its acceptance keeps the baseline selectable.
34	- A trivial step (pass-through, print) is built by its pipeline's wiring task; its stub names the wiring task.
35	
36	**Fakes and stubs.** A fake is allowed only where a pipeline calls another pipeline or package: both sides test against the same fixtures, so neither drifts from the contract unseen. Mocking a step is forbidden: a step's spine is the real baseline, and its consumer builds against the type's sample (for a Protocol or class, its replay double). The interface stubs are the floor's one stub carve-out (`build-setup`'s CR-02): allowed while the task they name is open; the gate fails on one naming a done task, the milestone exit on any.
37	
38	**Frozen means frozen.** Changing a frozen type, sample, signature or fake is a `contract-change`: `architecture-design` Revise, then a new interface task naming the symbol in `Read:`, never an edit to make a test pass. The bar enforces it (CR-09): once a contract folder exists at a task's base the implementer can't write it, and `dt guard` asks before any edit no open interface task covers. The re-freeze is that interface task, run by an implementer agent, never the main session: its `Writes:` names the frozen folders it changes, which opens them to it alone.
39

"""mdata's boundary types: CSV rows, aligned prices, and the gap error."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RawRow:
    """One CSV row: `date` is ISO yyyy-mm-dd; `close` is None for a blank cell."""

    date: str
    ticker: str
    close: float | None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Prices:
    """Aligned daily closes: dates ascending, tickers sorted, one positive close per date."""

    dates: tuple[str, ...]
    tickers: tuple[str, ...]
    closes: Mapping[str, tuple[float, ...]]


class GapError(ValueError):
    """A ticker misses more dates in a row than the calendar may fill."""

"""risk's boundary types: returns, volatility, the summary, the drawdown and the report."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Returns:
    """Daily log returns, dated after `base_date` (the prices' first date); one per date and ticker."""

    base_date: str
    dates: tuple[str, ...]
    tickers: tuple[str, ...]
    values: Mapping[str, tuple[float, ...]]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VolSeries:
    """Rolling volatility aligned to `returns.dates`; None until `window` earlier returns exist."""

    returns: Returns
    window: int
    annualized: bool
    values: Mapping[str, tuple[float | None, ...]]


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class TickerSummary:
    """One ticker's whole-period statistics."""

    ticker: str
    ann_return: float
    ann_vol: float
    sharpe: float
    last_vol: float | None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Drawdown:
    """One ticker's worst fall from a peak: `depth` <= 0."""

    ticker: str
    depth: float
    peak_date: str


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Report:
    """The written report: one row per ticker, sorted, the same rows as the file at `path`."""

    path: str
    rows: tuple[Mapping[str, object], ...]

D49Write/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-connorott-plugins-dev-team-workspace-iteration-b1-minilab-A0-r2-with-skill-project/4ab60903-9936-45df-9617-dc2940a6dfa8/scratchpad/samples.py (79 lines)
wrote /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-connorott-plugins-dev-team-workspace-iteration-b1-minilab-A0-r2-with-skill-project/4ab60903-9936-45df-9617-dc2940a6dfa8/scratchpad/samples.py
"""Write the M1 contract samples into tests/contracts/ (run from the project root)."""

import json
import math
import statistics
import sys
from pathlib import Path

root = Path(sys.argv[1]) / "tests" / "contracts"


def dump(name, payload):
    folder = root / name
    folder.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (folder / "sample.json").write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=1) + "\n")


rows = [
    ["2024-03-04", "AAA", 101.5],
    ["2024-03-04", "BBB", 48.2],
    ["2024-03-05", "AAA", 102.25],
    ["2024-03-05", "BBB", None],
    ["2024-03-06", "AAA", 101.0],
    ["2024-03-06", "BBB", 48.9],
    ["2024-03-07", "AAA", 103.4],
    ["2024-03-07", "BBB", 49.3],
]
dump("mdata.RawRow", {"rows": [{"date": d, "ticker": t, "close": c} for d, t, c in rows]})

dates = ["2024-03-04", "2024-03-05", "2024-03-06", "2024-03-07", "2024-03-08", "2024-03-11"]
closes = {
    "AAA": [101.5, 102.25, 101.0, 103.4, 104.1, 102.8],
    "BBB": [48.2, 48.6, 48.9, 49.3, 48.7, 49.5],
}
tickers = ["AAA", "BBB"]
prices = {"dates": dates, "tickers": tickers, "closes": closes}
dump("mdata.Prices", prices)

values = {t: [math.log(c[i] / c[i - 1]) for i in range(1, len(c))] for t, c in closes.items()}
returns = {"base_date": dates[0], "dates": dates[1:], "tickers": tickers, "values": values}
dump("risk.Returns", returns)

window = 2
vol = {
    t: [
        None if i < window else statistics.stdev(v[i - window : i]) * math.sqrt(252)
        for i in range(len(v))
    ]
    for t, v in values.items()
}
dump("risk.VolSeries", {"returns": returns, "window": window, "annualized": True, "values": vol})

r = values["AAA"]
ann_return = 252 * statistics.mean(r)
ann_vol = math.sqrt(252) * statistics.stdev(r)
summary = {
    "ticker": "AAA",
    "ann_return": ann_return,
    "ann_vol": ann_vol,
    "sharpe": ann_return / ann_vol,
    "last_vol": vol["AAA"][-1],
}
dump("risk.TickerSummary", summary)

wealth, peak, peak_date, depth, depth_peak = 1.0, 1.0, dates[0], 0.0, dates[0]
for d, x in zip(dates[1:], r, strict=True):
    wealth *= math.exp(x)
    if wealth > peak:
        peak, peak_date = wealth, d
    if wealth / peak - 1 < depth:
        depth, depth_peak = wealth / peak - 1, peak_date
drawdown = {"ticker": "AAA", "depth": depth, "peak_date": depth_peak}
dump("risk.Drawdown", drawdown)

row = {k: v for k, v in summary.items()} | {
    "max_drawdown": drawdown["depth"],
    "peak_date": drawdown["peak_date"],
}
dump("risk.Report", {"path": "reports/risk.json", "rows": [row]})

window : i]) * math.sqrt(252)\n        for i in range(len(v))\n    ]\n    for t, v in values.items()\n}\ndump(\"risk.VolSeries\", {\"returns\": returns, \"window\": window, \"annualized\": True, \"values\": vol})\n\nr = values[\"AAA\"]\nann_return = 252 * statistics.mean(r)\nann_vol = math.sqrt(252) * statistics.stdev(r)\nsummary = {\n    \"ticker\": \"AAA\",\n    \"ann_return\": ann_return,\n    \"ann_vol\": ann_vol,\n    \"sharpe\": ann_return / ann_vol,\n    \"last_vol\": vol[\"AAA\"][-1],\n}\ndump(\"risk.TickerSummary\", summary)\n\nwealth, peak, peak_date, depth, depth_peak = 1.0, 1.0, dates[0], 0.0, dates[0]\nfor d, x in zip(dates[1:], r, strict=True):\n    wealth *= math.exp(x)\n    if wealth > peak:\n        peak, peak_date = wealth, d\n    if wealth / peak - 1 < depth:\n        depth, depth_peak = wealth / peak - 1, peak_date\ndrawdown = {\"ticker\": \"AAA\", \"depth\": depth, \"peak_date\": depth_peak}\ndump(\"risk.Drawdown\", drawdown)\n\nrow = {k: v for k, v in summary.items()} | {\n    \"max_drawdown\": drawdown[\"depth\"],\n    \"peak_date\": drawdown[\"peak_date\"],\n}\ndump(\"risk.Report\", {\"path\": \"reports/risk.json\", \"rows\": [row]})\n"

"""Fake mdata.load_prices: serves the mdata.Prices sample (two tickers over six dates)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from collections.abc import Sequence
from pathlib import Path

from mdata import Prices

SAMPLE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "mdata.Prices" / "sample.json"


def load_prices(path: str | Path, tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> Prices:
    """The sample's prices, whatever the path; `tickers` narrows them, KeyError for an unknown one."""
    data = json.loads(SAMPLE.read_text())
    wanted = sorted(tickers) if tickers is not None else data["tickers"]
    for ticker in wanted:
        if ticker not in data["closes"]:
            raise KeyError(ticker)
    return Prices(
        dates=tuple(data["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(wanted),
        closes={t: tuple(data["closes"][t]) for t in wanted},
    )

"""Fake risk.vol_series: serves the risk.VolSeries sample (two tickers, window 2, annualized)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from collections.abc import Sequence
from pathlib import Path

from risk import Returns, VolSeries

SAMPLE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "risk.VolSeries" / "sample.json"


def vol_series(
    path: str | Path, *, window: int = 20, tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None
) -> VolSeries:
    """The sample's volatility, whatever the path and window; ValueError if `window` < 2."""
    if window < 2:
        raise ValueError(f"window must be at least 2, got {window}")
    data = json.loads(SAMPLE.read_text())
    r = data["returns"]
    wanted = sorted(tickers) if tickers is not None else r["tickers"]
    for ticker in wanted:
        if ticker not in r["values"]:
            raise KeyError(ticker)
    returns = Returns(
        base_date=r["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(r["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(wanted),
        values={t: tuple(r["values"][t]) for t in wanted},
    )
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=data["window"],
        annualized=data["annualized"],
        values={t: tuple(data["values"][t]) for t in wanted},
    )

"""The M1 contract: each sample is a valid instance of its type, each fake serves its sample."""

from __future__ import annotations

import inspect
import re

import mdata
import pytest
import risk
from mdata import Prices, RawRow
from risk import Drawdown, Report, Returns, TickerSummary, VolSeries

ISO_DATE = re.compile(r"\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}")


def test_exports_are_the_contract():
    assert sorted(mdata.__all__) == [
        "GapError",
        "Prices",
        "RawRow",
        "align_calendar",
        "load_prices",
        "read_csv",
        "validate_rows",
    ]
    assert sorted(risk.__all__) == [
        "Drawdown",
        "Report",
        "Returns",
        "TickerSummary",
        "VolSeries",
        "annualize",
        "log_returns",
        "max_drawdown",
        "risk_report",
        "rolling_vol",
        "summary_stats",
        "vol_series",
        "write_report",
    ]
    assert issubclass(mdata.GapError, ValueError)


def test_raw_row_sample(contract_sample):
    rows = [RawRow(**r) for r in contract_sample("mdata.RawRow")["rows"]]
    assert len(rows) == 8
    assert {r.ticker for r in rows} == {"AAA", "BBB"}
    assert all(ISO_DATE.fullmatch(r.date) for r in rows)
    assert [r.close for r in rows].count(None) == 1


def test_prices_sample(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("mdata.Prices")
    prices = Prices(
        dates=tuple(s["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(s["tickers"]),
        closes={t: tuple(c) for t, c in s["closes"].items()},
    )
    assert len(prices.dates) == 6
    assert list(prices.dates) == sorted(set(prices.dates))
    assert list(prices.tickers) == sorted(prices.closes)
    for closes in prices.closes.values():
        assert len(closes) == len(prices.dates)
        assert all(c > 0 for c in closes)


def check_returns(returns: Returns, prices_dates: list[str]) -> None:
    assert returns.base_date == prices_dates[0]
    assert list(returns.dates) == prices_dates[1:]
    assert list(returns.tickers) == sorted(returns.values)
    assert all(len(v) == len(returns.dates) for v in returns.values.values())


def returns_from(s: dict) -> Returns:
    return Returns(
        base_date=s["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(s["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(s["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()},
    )


def test_returns_sample(contract_sample):
    returns = returns_from(contract_sample("risk.Returns"))
    check_returns(returns, contract_sample("mdata.Prices")["dates"])


def test_vol_series_sample(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("risk.VolSeries")
    vol = VolSeries(
        returns=returns_from(s["returns"]),
        window=s["window"],
        annualized=s["annualized"],
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()},
    )
    assert vol.returns == returns_from(contract_sample("risk.Returns"))
    assert vol.window == 2
    assert vol.annualized
    for values in vol.values.values():
        assert len(values) == len(vol.returns.dates)
        assert values[: vol.window] == (None,) * vol.window
        assert all(v is not None and v >= 0 for v in values[vol.window :])


def test_ticker_summary_sample(contract_sample):
    summary = TickerSummary(**contract_sample("risk.TickerSummary"))
    assert summary.ticker in contract_sample("mdata.Prices")["tickers"]
    assert summary.ann_vol > 0
    assert summary.sharpe == pytest.approx(summary.ann_return / summary.ann_vol)
    assert summary.last_vol is not None


def test_drawdown_sample(contract_sample):
    drawdown = Drawdown(**contract_sample("risk.Drawdown"))
    assert drawdown.depth < 0
    assert drawdown.peak_date in contract_sample("mdata.Prices")["dates"]


def test_report_sample(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("risk.Report")
    report = Report(path=s["path"], rows=tuple(s["rows"]))
    tickers = [row["ticker"] for row in report.rows]
    assert tickers == sorted(set(tickers))
    summary = contract_sample("risk.TickerSummary")
    drawdown = contract_sample("risk.Drawdown")
    assert report.rows[0] == summary | {
        "max_drawdown": drawdown["depth"],
        "peak_date": drawdown["peak_date"],
    }


def test_load_prices_fake_serves_the_prices_sample(contract_fake, contract_sample):
    fake = contract_fake("mdata.load_prices")
    assert inspect.signature(fake.load_prices) == inspect.signature(mdata.load_prices)
    prices = fake.load_prices("any.csv")
    s = contract_sample("mdata.Prices")
    assert list(prices.dates) == s["dates"]
    assert {t: list(c) for t, c in prices.closes.items()} == s["closes"]
    assert fake.load_prices("any.csv", ["BBB"]).tickers == ("BBB",)
    with pytest.raises(KeyError, match="ZZZ"):
        fake.load_prices("any.csv", ["ZZZ"])


def test_vol_series_fake_serves_the_vol_sample(contract_fake, contract_sample):
    fake = contract_fake("risk.vol_series")
    assert inspect.signature(fake.vol_series) == inspect.signature(risk.vol_series)
    vol = fake.vol_series("any.csv", window=2)
    s = contract_sample("risk.VolSeries")
    assert vol.returns == returns_from(s["returns"])
    assert {t: list(v) for t, v in vol.values.items()} == s["values"]
    assert vol.annualized
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="window"):
        fake.vol_series("any.csv", window=1)

D81Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/io.py (33 lines)

"""PL-01 step 1: read the CSV's rows."""

from __future__ import annotations

import csv
from datetime import date
from pathlib import Path

from mdata.types import RawRow

COLUMNS = ("date", "ticker", "close")


def read_csv(path: str | Path) -> list[RawRow]:
    """Every `date,ticker,close` row in file order; a blank close is None."""
    with Path(path).open(newline="") as f:
        reader = csv.DictReader(f)
        missing = [c for c in COLUMNS if c not in (reader.fieldnames or ())]
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(f"{path}: missing column(s) {', '.join(missing)}")
        return [_row(line, reader.line_num) for line in reader]


def _row(line: dict[str, str], line_num: int) -> RawRow:
    day = line["date"].strip()
    try:
        date.fromisoformat(day)
    except ValueError:
        raise ValueError(f"line {line_num}: malformed date {day!r}") from None
    if len(day) != len("yyyy-mm-dd"):
        raise ValueError(f"line {line_num}: malformed date {day!r}")
    close = line["close"].strip()
    return RawRow(date=day, ticker=line["ticker"].strip(), close=float(close) if close else None)

def _is_iso_date(text: str) -> bool:
    if not ISO_DATE.fullmatch(text):
        return False
    try:
        date.fromisoformat(text)
    except ValueError:
        return False
    return True

"""read_csv: every row in file order, a blank close missing (DATA-01)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import RawRow, read_csv


def write(tmp_path, text: str):
    path = tmp_path / "prices.csv"
    path.write_text(text)
    return path


def test_reads_every_row_in_file_order(tmp_path):
    path = write(
        tmp_path,
        "date,ticker,close\n2024-03-05,BBB,48.6\n2024-03-04,AAA,101.5\n2024-03-04,BBB,\n",
    )
    assert read_csv(path) == [
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "BBB", 48.6),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 101.5),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", None),
    ]


def test_blank_close_is_missing_not_zero(tmp_path):
    rows = read_csv(write(tmp_path, "date,ticker,close\n2024-03-04,AAA, \n"))
    assert rows[0].close is None


def test_columns_in_any_order_and_str_path(tmp_path):
    path = write(tmp_path, "ticker,close,date\nAAA,0,2024-03-04\n")
    assert read_csv(str(path)) == [RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 0.0)]


def test_missing_column_is_a_value_error(tmp_path):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="close"):
        read_csv(write(tmp_path, "date,ticker\n2024-03-04,AAA\n"))


@pytest.mark.parametrize("day", ["03/04/2024", "20240304", "2024-02-30", ""])
def test_malformed_date_is_a_value_error(tmp_path, day):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="malformed date"):
        read_csv(write(tmp_path, f"date,ticker,close\n{day},AAA,1.0\n"))
full input and output
input

"""PL-01 step 2: keep the usable rows, sorted."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence

from mdata.types import RawRow


def validate_rows(rows: Sequence[RawRow], tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> list[RawRow]:
    """Positive closes only, one row per (date, ticker), sorted by (date, ticker)."""
    kept = [r for r in rows if r.close is not None and (tickers is None or r.ticker in tickers)]
    return sorted(kept, key=lambda r: (r.date, r.ticker))

"""validate_rows: rows with a close, sorted by (date, ticker)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from mdata import RawRow, validate_rows


def test_sample_keeps_rows_with_a_close_sorted(contract_sample):
    rows = [RawRow(**r) for r in contract_sample("mdata.RawRow")["rows"]]
    kept = validate_rows(rows)
    assert len(kept) == 7
    assert RawRow("2024-03-05", "BBB", None) not in kept
    assert kept == sorted(kept, key=lambda r: (r.date, r.ticker))


def test_sorts_by_date_then_ticker():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 2.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 3.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0),
    ]
    assert validate_rows(rows) == [
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 3.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 2.0),
    ]


def test_keeps_only_requested_tickers():
    rows = [RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0), RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 3.0)]
    assert validate_rows(rows, ["BBB"]) == [RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 3.0)]

D101Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py (22 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py
"""PL-01 step 3: put every ticker on one calendar."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence

from mdata.types import Prices, RawRow


def align_calendar(rows: Sequence[RawRow], *, max_gap: int = 5) -> Prices:
    """One calendar from the first date every ticker has a close; gaps take the previous close."""
    closes: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
    for r in rows:
        if r.close is not None:
            closes.setdefault(r.ticker, {})[r.date] = r.close
    tickers = sorted(closes)
    dates = sorted(set.intersection(*(set(c) for c in closes.values()))) if closes else []
    return Prices(
        dates=tuple(dates),
        tickers=tuple(tickers),
        closes={t: tuple(closes[t][d] for d in dates) for t in tickers},
    )

"""align_calendar: the dates every ticker has."""

from __future__ import annotations

from mdata import Prices, RawRow, align_calendar


def test_sample_gives_the_dates_both_tickers_have(contract_sample):
    rows = [RawRow(**r) for r in contract_sample("mdata.RawRow")["rows"]]
    prices = align_calendar(rows)
    assert prices.tickers == ("AAA", "BBB")
    assert prices.dates == ("2024-03-04", "2024-03-06", "2024-03-07")
    assert prices.closes == {"AAA": (101.5, 101.0, 103.4), "BBB": (48.2, 48.9, 49.3)}


def test_dates_ascending_tickers_sorted():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "BBB", 2.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 4.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 1.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 3.0),
    ]
    assert align_calendar(rows) == Prices(
        dates=("2024-03-04", "2024-03-05"),
        tickers=("AAA", "BBB"),
        closes={"AAA": (3.0, 4.0), "BBB": (1.0, 2.0)},
    )


def test_no_rows_is_an_empty_calendar():
    assert align_calendar([]) == Prices(dates=(), tickers=(), closes={})

## PL-02: Volatility
**Entry:** `packages/risk/src/risk/vol_pipeline.py::vol_series` · **Trigger:** called from Python (`risk.vol_series`)
**Covers:** RISK-01–RISK-03

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `load_prices` | mdata | path → Prices | PL-01 as built | DATA-01 |
| 2 | `log_returns` | risk.returns | Prices → Returns | simple returns | RISK-01 |
| 3 | `rolling_vol` | risk.vol | Returns → VolSeries | expanding stdev of earlier returns | RISK-02 |
| 4 | `annualize` | risk.annualize | VolSeries → VolSeries | daily values pass through | RISK-03 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def vol_series(path, *, window=20, tickers=None) -> VolSeries:
    prices = load_prices(path, tickers)
    returns = log_returns(prices)
    vol = rolling_vol(returns, window)
    return annualize(vol)
```

### mdata.Prices: Aligned daily closes
**Kind:** type · **Sample:** `tests/contracts/mdata.Prices/sample.json` · **Cases:** two tickers over six aligned dates
`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Prices: dates: tuple[str, ...]; tickers: tuple[str, ...]; closes: Mapping[str, tuple[float, ...]]`
- **Invariants:** dates ascending; tickers sorted; every ticker has one positive close per date
- **Used by:** PL-01, PL-02

<!-- docs/packages/risk/contract.md:7 -->
### risk.Returns: Daily log returns
**Kind:** type · **Sample:** `tests/contracts/risk.Returns/sample.json` · **Cases:** two tickers' log returns over five dates
`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Returns: base_date: str; dates: tuple[str, ...]; tickers: tuple[str, ...]; values: Mapping[str, tuple[float, ...]]`
- **Invariants:** `dates` are the prices' dates after the first, which is `base_date`; one value per date and ticker
- **Used by:** PL-02, PL-03

## Layout (`dt layout risk`)
packages/risk/src/risk/
  __init__.py — risk: returns, volatility and the report, over mdata's prices.
  annualize.py — PL-02 step 4: annualize daily volatility over trading days.
  drawdown.py — PL-03 step 3: each ticker's maximum drawdown.
  report.py — PL-03 step 4: write the JSON report.
  report_pipeline.py — PL-03: Risk report.
  returns.py — PL-02 step 2: daily returns from closes.
  summary.py — PL-03 step 2: each ticker's whole-period summary.
  types.py — risk's boundary types: returns, volatility, the summary, the drawdown and the report.
  vol.py — PL-02 step 3: rolling volatility from earlier returns.
  vol_pipeline.py — PL-02: Volatility.

D109Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/returns.py (19 lines)

"""PL-02 step 2: daily returns from closes."""

from __future__ import annotations

from mdata.types import Prices
from risk.types import Returns


def log_returns(prices: Prices) -> Returns:
    """r_t = ln(close_t / close_t-1), dated t."""
    return Returns(
        base_date=prices.dates[0],
        dates=prices.dates[1:],
        tickers=prices.tickers,
        values={
            t: tuple(c / p - 1 for p, c in zip(closes, closes[1:], strict=False))
            for t, closes in prices.closes.items()
        },
    )

"""log_returns: one return per date after the first, per ticker."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import Prices
from risk import log_returns


def prices_from(s: dict) -> Prices:
    return Prices(
        dates=tuple(s["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(s["tickers"]),
        closes={t: tuple(c) for t, c in s["closes"].items()},
    )


def test_sample_dates_after_the_base_date(contract_sample):
    prices = prices_from(contract_sample("mdata.Prices"))
    returns = log_returns(prices)
    assert returns.base_date == "2024-03-04"
    assert returns.dates == prices.dates[1:]
    assert returns.tickers == ("AAA", "BBB")
    assert {t: len(v) for t, v in returns.values.items()} == {"AAA": 5, "BBB": 5}


def test_a_doubling_close_is_a_positive_return_a_flat_one_zero():
    prices = Prices(
        dates=("2024-03-04", "2024-03-05"),
        tickers=("AAA", "BBB"),
        closes={"AAA": (10.0, 20.0), "BBB": (5.0, 5.0)},
    )
    values = log_returns(prices).values
    assert values["AAA"][0] > 0
    assert values["BBB"] == (pytest.approx(0.0),)

D122Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/vol.py (20 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/vol.py
"""PL-02 step 3: rolling volatility from earlier returns."""

from __future__ import annotations

from statistics import stdev

from risk.types import Returns, VolSeries


def rolling_vol(returns: Returns, window: int) -> VolSeries:
    """Sample stdev of the `window` returns before each date, never the date's own."""
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=window,
        annualized=False,
        values={
            t: tuple(None if i < window else stdev(v[:i]) for i in range(len(v)))
            for t, v in returns.values.items()
        },
    )

wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/unit/risk/test_vol.py
"""rolling_vol: volatility from earlier returns only, None until `window` of them exist."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, rolling_vol


def returns_of(values: dict[str, tuple[float, ...]]) -> Returns:
    n = len(next(iter(values.values())))
    dates = tuple(f"2024-03-{d:02d}" for d in range(5, 5 + n))
    return Returns("2024-03-04", dates, tuple(sorted(values)), values)


def test_sample_is_aligned_and_daily(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("risk.Returns")
    returns = Returns(
        s["base_date"],
        tuple(s["dates"]),
        tuple(s["tickers"]),
        {t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()},
    )
    vol = rolling_vol(returns, 2)
    assert vol.returns is returns
    assert vol.window == 2
    assert not vol.annualized
    for values in vol.values.values():
        assert len(values) == 5
        assert values[:2] == (None, None)
        assert all(v is not None and v > 0 for v in values[2:])


def test_uses_earlier_returns_never_the_dates_own():
    # The third return is huge: only the date after it may see it.
    vol = rolling_vol(returns_of({"AAA": (0.01, 0.03, 9.0, 0.0)}), 2)
    assert vol.values["AAA"][2] == pytest.approx(0.01414213562373095)
    assert vol.values["AAA"][3] > 1


def test_constant_returns_have_zero_volatility():
    vol = rolling_vol(returns_of({"AAA": (0.01,) * 5}), 3)
    assert vol.values["AAA"] == (None, None, None, 0.0, 0.0)

```python
def vol_series(path, *, window=20, tickers=None) -> VolSeries:
    prices = load_prices(path, tickers)
    returns = log_returns(prices)
```

"""PL-02 step 4: annualize daily volatility over trading days."""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import replace

from risk.types import VolSeries


def annualize(vol: VolSeries, periods_per_year: int = 252) -> VolSeries:
    """Each daily value times sqrt(periods_per_year); gaps stay None."""
    return replace(vol, annualized=True)

"""annualize: an annualized series, aligned to the same returns, gaps kept."""

from __future__ import annotations

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, annualize

RETURNS = Returns(
    "2024-03-04",
    ("2024-03-05", "2024-03-06", "2024-03-07"),
    ("AAA",),
    {"AAA": (0.01, -0.02, 0.005)},
)


def test_daily_series_comes_back_annualized_on_the_same_returns():
    daily = VolSeries(RETURNS, 2, False, {"AAA": (None, None, 0.02)})
    vol = annualize(daily)
    assert vol.annualized
    assert vol.returns is RETURNS
    assert vol.window == 2
    assert vol.values["AAA"][:2] == (None, None)
    assert vol.values["AAA"][2] is not None

D140Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/summary.py (25 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/summary.py
"""PL-03 step 2: each ticker's whole-period summary."""

from __future__ import annotations

from statistics import mean, stdev

from risk.types import TickerSummary, VolSeries


def summary_stats(vol: VolSeries, periods_per_year: int = 252) -> tuple[TickerSummary, ...]:
    """Annualized return, volatility and Sharpe per ticker, with its last rolling volatility."""
    summaries = []
    for ticker in vol.returns.tickers:
        returns = vol.returns.values[ticker]
        ret, sd = mean(returns), stdev(returns)
        summaries.append(
            TickerSummary(
                ticker=ticker,
                ann_return=ret,
                ann_vol=sd,
                sharpe=ret / sd if sd else 0.0,
                last_vol=vol.values[ticker][-1],
            )
        )
    return tuple(summaries)

"""summary_stats: one summary per ticker, in ticker order, with its last rolling volatility."""

from __future__ import annotations

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, summary_stats


def vol_from(s: dict) -> VolSeries:
    r = s["returns"]
    returns = Returns(
        r["base_date"],
        tuple(r["dates"]),
        tuple(r["tickers"]),
        {t: tuple(v) for t, v in r["values"].items()},
    )
    return VolSeries(returns, s["window"], s["annualized"], {t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()})


def test_sample_gives_one_summary_per_ticker(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("risk.VolSeries")
    summary = summary_stats(vol_from(s))
    assert [x.ticker for x in summary] == ["AAA", "BBB"]
    assert [x.last_vol for x in summary] == [s["values"]["AAA"][-1], s["values"]["BBB"][-1]]
    for x in summary:
        assert x.ann_vol > 0


def test_zero_volatility_has_zero_sharpe():
    returns = Returns("2024-03-04", ("2024-03-05", "2024-03-06"), ("AAA",), {"AAA": (0.01, 0.01)})
    (summary,) = summary_stats(VolSeries(returns, 2, True, {"AAA": (None, None)}))
    assert summary.ann_vol == 0
    assert summary.sharpe == 0
    assert summary.ann_return > 0
    assert summary.last_vol is None

```python
def risk_report(path, out_path, *, window=20, tickers=None) -> Report:
    vol = vol_series(path, window=window, tickers=tickers)
    summary = summary_stats(vol)
    drawdowns = max_drawdown(vol.returns)
    return write_report(summary, drawdowns, out_path)
```

D150Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/drawdown.py (19 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/drawdown.py
"""PL-03 step 3: each ticker's maximum drawdown."""

from __future__ import annotations

from itertools import accumulate
from math import exp

from risk.types import Drawdown, Returns


def max_drawdown(returns: Returns) -> tuple[Drawdown, ...]:
    """The deepest fall below the running peak of wealth, which starts at 1 on `base_date`."""
    drawdowns = []
    for ticker in returns.tickers:
        wealth = [1.0, *(exp(x) for x in accumulate(returns.values[ticker]))]
        peaks = accumulate(wealth, max)
        depth = min(w / p - 1 for w, p in zip(wealth, peaks, strict=True))
        drawdowns.append(Drawdown(ticker=ticker, depth=depth, peak_date=returns.base_date))
    return tuple(drawdowns)

D151Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/unit/risk/test_drawdown.py (37 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/unit/risk/test_drawdown.py
"""max_drawdown: the deepest fall of wealth below its running peak, at most 0."""

from __future__ import annotations

from math import log

import pytest

from risk import Returns, max_drawdown


def test_sample_gives_one_drawdown_per_ticker(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("risk.Returns")
    returns = Returns(
        s["base_date"],
        tuple(s["dates"]),
        tuple(s["tickers"]),
        {t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()},
    )
    drawdowns = max_drawdown(returns)
    assert [d.ticker for d in drawdowns] == ["AAA", "BBB"]
    assert all(d.depth < 0 for d in drawdowns)


def test_depth_is_trough_over_peak_minus_one():
    # Closes 100, 110, 99, 88, 95: peak 110, trough 88.
    closes = (100, 110, 99, 88, 95)
    values = tuple(log(b / a) for a, b in zip(closes, closes[1:], strict=False))
    dates = ("2024-03-05", "2024-03-06", "2024-03-07", "2024-03-08")
    (dd,) = max_drawdown(Returns("2024-03-04", dates, ("AAA",), {"AAA": values}))
    assert dd.depth == pytest.approx(88 / 110 - 1, abs=1e-12)


def test_rising_wealth_has_no_drawdown():
    returns = Returns("2024-03-04", ("2024-03-05", "2024-03-06"), ("AAA",), {"AAA": (0.01, 0.02)})
    (dd,) = max_drawdown(returns)
    assert dd.depth == 0

```python
def risk_report(path, out_path, *, window=20, tickers=None) -> Report:
    vol = vol_series(path, window=window, tickers=tickers)
    summary = summary_stats(vol)
    drawdowns = max_drawdown(vol.returns)
```

<!-- docs/spec.md:40 -->
- **REP-01** The risk report is a JSON file.
  - Given a prices CSV, when the report runs, then `{"tickers": [...]}` is written with one row per ticker, sorted, holding the RISK-04 fields and RISK-05's drawdown (as `max_drawdown`) and both dates.

D162Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/report.py (27 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/report.py
"""PL-03 step 4: write the JSON report."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import asdict
from pathlib import Path

from risk.types import Drawdown, Report, TickerSummary


def write_report(
    summary: Sequence[TickerSummary], drawdowns: Sequence[Drawdown], out_path: str | Path
) -> Report:
    """Write `{"tickers": [...]}`, one row per ticker, sorted."""
    by_ticker = {d.ticker: d for d in drawdowns}
    if sorted(by_ticker) != sorted(s.ticker for s in summary):
        raise ValueError("summary and drawdowns cover different tickers")
    rows = tuple(
        asdict(s) | {"max_drawdown": by_ticker[s.ticker].depth, "peak_date": by_ticker[s.ticker].peak_date}
        for s in sorted(summary, key=lambda s: s.ticker)
    )
    path = Path(out_path)
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path.write_text(json.dumps({"tickers": rows}, indent=2) + "\n")
    return Report(path=str(path), rows=rows)

D163Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/unit/risk/test_report.py (48 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/unit/risk/test_report.py
"""write_report: `{"tickers": [...]}` on disk, one row per ticker, sorted (REP-01)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json

import pytest

from risk import Drawdown, TickerSummary, write_report


def test_sample_row_is_written_and_returned(contract_sample, tmp_path):
    summary = TickerSummary(**contract_sample("risk.TickerSummary"))
    drawdown = Drawdown(**contract_sample("risk.Drawdown"))
    out = tmp_path / "reports" / "risk.json"
    report = write_report([summary], [drawdown], out)
    assert report.path == str(out)
    assert list(report.rows) == contract_sample("risk.Report")["rows"]
    assert json.loads(out.read_text()) == {"tickers": list(report.rows)}


def test_rows_sorted_by_ticker(tmp_path):
    summary = [
        TickerSummary("BBB", 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, None),
        TickerSummary("AAA", 0.3, 0.1, 3.0, 0.12),
    ]
    drawdowns = [Drawdown("AAA", -0.1, "2024-03-05"), Drawdown("BBB", 0.0, "2024-03-04")]
    report = write_report(summary, drawdowns, tmp_path / "r.json")
    assert [r["ticker"] for r in report.rows] == ["AAA", "BBB"]
    assert report.rows[1]["max_drawdown"] == 0.0
    assert report.rows[1]["last_vol"] is None


def test_same_inputs_same_file(tmp_path):
    summary = [TickerSummary("AAA", 0.3, 0.1, 3.0, 0.12)]
    drawdowns = [Drawdown("AAA", -0.1, "2024-03-05")]
    write_report(summary, drawdowns, tmp_path / "a.json")
    write_report(summary, drawdowns, tmp_path / "b.json")
    assert (tmp_path / "a.json").read_bytes() == (tmp_path / "b.json").read_bytes()


def test_different_tickers_is_a_value_error(tmp_path):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="different tickers"):
        write_report(
            [TickerSummary("AAA", 0.3, 0.1, 3.0, 0.12)],
            [Drawdown("BBB", -0.1, "2024-03-05")],
            tmp_path / "r.json",
        )

```python
def load_prices(path, tickers=None) -> Prices:
    rows = read_csv(path)
    rows = validate_rows(rows, tickers)
    return align_calendar(rows)
```

<!-- docs/packages/mdata/contract.md:33 -->
### mdata.read_csv: Read the rows
`def read_csv(path: str | Path) -> list[RawRow]`
- **Errors:** `ValueError` for a missing column or a malformed date
- **Used by:** PL-01

<!-- docs/packages/mdata/contract.md:38 -->
### mdata.validate_rows: Usable rows, sorted
`def validate_rows(rows: Sequence[RawRow], tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> list[RawRow]`
- **Invariants:** positive closes only; one row per (date, ticker), the later one in file order; sorted by (date, ticker)
- **Errors:** `KeyError` for a requested ticker with no usable row
- **Used by:** PL-01

<!-- docs/packages/mdata/contract.md:44 -->
### mdata.align_calendar: One calendar
`def align_calendar(rows: Sequence[RawRow], *, max_gap: int = 5) -> Prices`
- **Invariants:** starts on the first date every ticker has a close; gaps take the previous close
- **Errors:** `GapError` naming the ticker when more than `max_gap` dates in a row are missing
- **Used by:** PL-01

## Layout (`dt layout mdata`)
packages/mdata/src/mdata/
  __init__.py — mdata: daily closes from a prices CSV, on one calendar.
  calendar.py — PL-01 step 3: put every ticker on one calendar.
  io.py — PL-01 step 1: read the CSV's rows.
  pipeline.py — PL-01: Load prices.
  types.py — mdata's boundary types: CSV rows, aligned prices, and the gap error.
  validate.py — PL-01 step 2: keep the usable rows, sorted.

"""PL-01: Load prices."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence
from pathlib import Path

from mdata.calendar import align_calendar
from mdata.io import read_csv
from mdata.types import Prices
from mdata.validate import validate_rows


def load_prices(path: str | Path, tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> Prices:
    """Aligned closes from a `date,ticker,close` CSV."""
    rows = read_csv(path)
    rows = validate_rows(rows, tickers)
    return align_calendar(rows)

"""PL-01 smoke: load_prices runs end to end on a CSV; checks shape, never values."""

from __future__ import annotations

from mdata import Prices, load_prices

CSV = """date,ticker,close
2024-03-04,AAA,101.5
2024-03-04,BBB,48.2
2024-03-05,AAA,102.25
2024-03-05,BBB,48.6
2024-03-06,AAA,101.0
2024-03-06,BBB,48.9
2024-03-07,AAA,103.4
2024-03-07,BBB,49.3
"""


def test_load_prices_end_to_end(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "prices.csv"
    path.write_text(CSV)
    prices = load_prices(path)
    assert isinstance(prices, Prices)
    assert prices.tickers == ("AAA", "BBB")
    assert len(prices.dates) == 4
    assert {t: len(c) for t, c in prices.closes.items()} == {"AAA": 4, "BBB": 4}


def test_load_prices_requested_tickers(tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "prices.csv"
    path.write_text(CSV)
    assert load_prices(str(path), ["BBB"]).tickers == ("BBB",)

## PL-02: Volatility
**Entry:** `packages/risk/src/risk/vol_pipeline.py::vol_series` · **Trigger:** called from Python (`risk.vol_series`)
**Covers:** RISK-01–RISK-03

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `load_prices` | mdata | path → Prices | PL-01 as built | DATA-01 |
| 2 | `log_returns` | risk.returns | Prices → Returns | simple returns | RISK-01 |
| 3 | `rolling_vol` | risk.vol | Returns → VolSeries | expanding stdev of earlier returns | RISK-02 |
| 4 | `annualize` | risk.annualize | VolSeries → VolSeries | daily values pass through | RISK-03 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def vol_series(path, *, window=20, tickers=None) -> VolSeries:
    prices = load_prices(path, tickers)
    returns = log_returns(prices)
    vol = rolling_vol(returns, window)
    return annualize(vol)
```

### mdata.load_prices: Prices from a CSV
`def load_prices(path: str | Path, tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> Prices`
- **Invariants:** DATA-01–DATA-04 hold for the result
- **Errors:** `KeyError` for a requested ticker with no usable row; `GapError` for a gap over 5 dates; `ValueError` for a malformed file
- **Boundary:** risk · **Fixtures:** tests/contracts/mdata.load_prices/ · **Cases:** two tickers over six dates
- **Used by:** risk; PL-01, PL-02

## Layout (`dt layout risk`)
packages/risk/src/risk/
  __init__.py — risk: returns, volatility and the report, over mdata's prices.
  annualize.py — PL-02 step 4: annualize daily volatility over trading days.
  drawdown.py — PL-03 step 3: each ticker's maximum drawdown.
  report.py — PL-03 step 4: write the JSON report.
  report_pipeline.py — PL-03: Risk report.
  returns.py — PL-02 step 2: daily returns from closes.
  summary.py — PL-03 step 2: each ticker's whole-period summary.
  types.py — risk's boundary types: returns, volatility, the summary, the drawdown and the report.
  vol.py — PL-02 step 3: rolling volatility from earlier returns.
  vol_pipeline.py — PL-02: Volatility.

"""PL-02: Volatility."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence
from pathlib import Path

from mdata import load_prices
from risk.annualize import annualize
from risk.returns import log_returns
from risk.types import VolSeries
from risk.vol import rolling_vol


def vol_series(
    path: str | Path, *, window: int = 20, tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None
) -> VolSeries:
    """Annualized rolling volatility of a prices CSV."""
    prices = load_prices(path, tickers)
    returns = log_returns(prices)
    vol = rolling_vol(returns, window)
    return annualize(vol)

"""PL-02 smoke: vol_series runs end to end on the mdata.load_prices fake; checks shape, never values."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

import risk.vol_pipeline
from risk import VolSeries, vol_series


@pytest.fixture
def fake_prices(monkeypatch, contract_fake):
    monkeypatch.setattr(
        risk.vol_pipeline, "load_prices", contract_fake("mdata.load_prices").load_prices
    )


@pytest.mark.usefixtures("fake_prices")
def test_vol_series_end_to_end():
    vol = vol_series("prices.csv", window=2)
    assert isinstance(vol, VolSeries)
    assert vol.annualized
    assert vol.window == 2
    assert vol.returns.tickers == ("AAA", "BBB")
    assert len(vol.returns.dates) == 5
    assert {t: len(v) for t, v in vol.values.items()} == {"AAA": 5, "BBB": 5}


@pytest.mark.usefixtures("fake_prices")
def test_vol_series_requested_tickers():
    vol = vol_series("prices.csv", window=2, tickers=["BBB"])
    assert vol.returns.tickers == ("BBB",)
    assert list(vol.values) == ["BBB"]

## PL-03: Risk report

| 3 | `max_drawdown` | risk.drawdown | Returns → Drawdown | depth only | RISK-05 |
| 4 | `write_report` | risk.report | TickerSummary, Drawdown → Report | JSON rows | REP-01 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def risk_report(path, out_path, *, window=20, tickers=None) -> Report:
    vol = vol_series(path, window=window, tickers=tickers)
    summary = summary_stats(vol)
    drawdowns = max_drawdown(vol.returns)
    return write_report(summary, drawdowns, out_path)
```

## Layout (`dt layout risk`)
packages/risk/src/risk/
  __init__.py — risk: returns, volatility and the report, over mdata's prices.
  annualize.py — PL-02 step 4: annualize daily volatility over trading days.
  drawdown.py — PL-03 step 3: each ticker's maximum drawdown.
  report.py — PL-03 step 4: write the JSON report.
  report_pipeline.py — PL-03: Risk report.
  returns.py — PL-02 step 2: daily returns from closes.
  summary.py — PL-03 step 2: each ticker's whole-period summary.
  types.py — risk's boundary types: returns, volatility, the summary, the drawdown and the report.
  vol.py — PL-02 step 3: rolling volatility from earlier returns.
  vol_pipeline.py — PL-02: Volatility.

D197Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/report_pipeline.py (26 lines)

"""PL-03: Risk report."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence
from pathlib import Path

from risk.drawdown import max_drawdown
from risk.report import write_report
from risk.summary import summary_stats
from risk.types import Report
from risk.vol_pipeline import vol_series


def risk_report(
    path: str | Path,
    out_path: str | Path,
    *,
    window: int = 20,
    tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None,
) -> Report:
    """The JSON risk report of a prices CSV, written to `out_path`."""
    vol = vol_series(path, window=window, tickers=tickers)
    summary = summary_stats(vol)
    drawdowns = max_drawdown(vol.returns)
    return write_report(summary, drawdowns, out_path)

"""PL-03 smoke: risk_report runs end to end on the risk.vol_series fake; checks shape, never values."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json

import pytest

import risk.report_pipeline
from risk import Report, risk_report


@pytest.fixture
def fake_vol(monkeypatch, contract_fake):
    monkeypatch.setattr(
        risk.report_pipeline, "vol_series", contract_fake("risk.vol_series").vol_series
    )


@pytest.mark.usefixtures("fake_vol")
def test_risk_report_end_to_end(tmp_path):
    out = tmp_path / "report.json"
    report = risk_report("prices.csv", out, window=2)
    assert isinstance(report, Report)
    assert report.path == str(out)
    assert [row["ticker"] for row in report.rows] == ["AAA", "BBB"]
    assert json.loads(out.read_text()) == {"tickers": list(report.rows)}


@pytest.mark.usefixtures("fake_vol")
def test_risk_report_requested_tickers(tmp_path):
    report = risk_report("prices.csv", tmp_path / "report.json", window=2, tickers=["AAA"])
    assert [row["ticker"] for row in report.rows] == ["AAA"]
