## PL-01: Load prices
**Entry:** `packages/mdata/src/mdata/pipeline.py::load_prices` · **Trigger:** called from Python (`mdata.load_prices`)
**Covers:** DATA-01–DATA-04, QR-01

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `read_csv` | mdata.io | path → RawRow | read every row | DATA-01 |
| 2 | `validate_rows` | mdata.validate | RawRow → RawRow | keep rows with a close, sorted | DATA-02 |
| 3 | `align_calendar` | mdata.calendar | RawRow → Prices | dates every ticker has, no fill | DATA-03, DATA-04 |

Layout: `dt layout mdata`

<!-- docs/packages/mdata/contract.md:38 -->
### mdata.validate_rows: Usable rows, sorted
`def validate_rows(rows: Sequence[RawRow], tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> list[RawRow]`
- **Invariants:** positive closes only; one row per (date, ticker), the later one in file order; sorted by (date, ticker)
- **Errors:** `KeyError` for a requested ticker with no usable row
- **Used by:** PL-01

<!-- docs/packages/mdata/contract.md:7 -->
### mdata.RawRow: One CSV row
**Kind:** type · **Sample:** `tests/contracts/mdata.RawRow/sample.json` · **Cases:** eight rows of two tickers, one with a blank close
`@dataclass(frozen=True) class RawRow: date: str; ticker: str; close: float | None`
- **Invariants:** `date` is ISO `yyyy-mm-dd`; `close` is None for a blank cell
- **Used by:** PL-01

<!-- docs/packages/mdata/tests.md:10 -->
### TC-01: Unusable rows and duplicates
**Pipeline:** PL-01 · **Covers:** DATA-02 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/mdata/TC-01/
**Source:** nine hand-made rows: a blank, a zero and a negative close, one repeated (date, ticker)
**Expected:** five rows, the later duplicate kept, sorted by date then ticker · **Tolerance:** exact

<!-- docs/spec.md:13 -->
- **DATA-02** Unusable rows are dropped and duplicates resolved.
  - Given rows with blank, zero or negative closes and a repeated (date, ticker), when validated, then those rows are gone, the later of the duplicates wins, and rows are sorted by date, then ticker.
  - Given a requested ticker with no usable row, when validated, then a `KeyError` names it.

## Layout (`dt layout mdata`)
packages/mdata/src/mdata/
  __init__.py — mdata: daily closes from a prices CSV, on one calendar.
  calendar.py — PL-01 step 3: put every ticker on one calendar.
  io.py — PL-01 step 1: read the CSV's rows.
  pipeline.py — PL-01: Load prices.
  types.py — mdata's boundary types: CSV rows, aligned prices, and the gap error.
  validate.py — PL-01 step 2: keep the usable rows, sorted.

## Writes (edit only these files; the plan gives every other file to another task)
- packages/mdata/src/mdata/validate.py (exists)
- tests/intent/mdata/test_validate.py (new)
- tests/unit/mdata/test_validate.py (exists)

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""RED for the right reason: the tester's new intent tests fail on the behaviour, not the wiring.
3	
4	Called by `scripts/check.sh collect` with the intent test files changed since the task's base.
5	Runs them once and sorts every test by why it ended:
6	
7	    right  AssertionError, NotImplementedError (an interface stub, `raise
8	           NotImplementedError("M1-T05")`) or pytest.fail / pytest.raises' "DID NOT RAISE"
9	    wrong  anything else: ImportError, AttributeError, NameError, TypeError, a fixture or
10	           collection error. The test can't reach the behaviour it claims to check.
11	    passed the code already does this; listed, not a failure
12	
13	Exits 1 when any test is wrong, or when none is red: a test that can't fail proves nothing.
14	With no changed test file there is nothing to judge, and it exits 0.
15	Prints one line per test that is not right, as `nodeid: reason`.
16	"""
17	
18	from __future__ import annotations
19	
20	import importlib
21	import sys
22	from typing import Any
23	
24	RIGHT = {"AssertionError", "NotImplementedError", "Failed"}  # Failed: pytest.fail, DID NOT RAISE
25	
26	
27	def _right(exc: BaseException | None) -> bool:
28	    return exc is not None and bool({c.__name__ for c in type(exc).__mro__} & RIGHT)
29	
30	
31	class Recorder:
32	    """A pytest plugin that keeps how each test ended: right, wrong (with why) or passed."""
33	
34	    def __init__(self) -> None:
35	        self.results: dict[str, str] = {}
36	
37	    def pytest_collectreport(self, report: Any) -> None:
38	        if report.failed:
39	            self.results[report.nodeid or "collection"] = "wrong: collection error"
40	
41	    def pytest_exception_interact(self, node: Any, call: Any, report: Any) -> None:
42	        if not hasattr(node, "function"):
43	            return  # a collector: pytest_collectreport has it
44	        exc = call.excinfo.value if call.excinfo else None
45	        if call.when == "call" and _right(exc):
46	            self.results[node.nodeid] = "right"
47	        else:
48	            self.results[node.nodeid] = f"wrong: {call.when}: {type(exc).__name__}"
49	
50	    def pytest_runtest_logreport(self, report: Any) -> None:
51	        if report.when == "call" and report.passed:
52	            self.results.setdefault(report.nodeid, "passed")
53	
54	
55	def main(paths: list[str]) -> int:
56	    if not paths:
57	        print("red_check: no intent test changed since the base; nothing to check", file=sys.stderr)
58	        return 0
59	    # pytest is a dev dependency, present wherever check.sh runs. Imported by name: deptry audits
60	    # the product's imports, and this script is the bar's.
61	    pytest = importlib.import_module("pytest")
62	    recorder = Recorder()
63	    # -m "": every marker, so a slow or perf intent test the default addopts deselect is judged too.
64	    pytest.main(
65	        [*paths, "-q", "--no-header", "--no-cov", "-m", "", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"], [recorder]
66	    )
67	    results = recorder.results
68	    wrong = {n: r for n, r in results.items() if r.startswith("wrong")}
69	    for nodeid, why in sorted(wrong.items()):
70	        print(f"{nodeid}: fails for the wrong reason ({why.removeprefix('wrong: ')})")
71	    for nodeid in sorted(n for n, r in results.items() if r == "passed"):
72	        print(f"{nodeid}: passes already (not red)")
73	    if wrong:
74	        print("red_check: fix the tests above: each must reach the code and fail on its behaviour")
75	        return 1
76	    red = sum(r == "right" for r in results.values())
77	    if not red:
78	        print("red_check: no test is red; a test that can't fail proves nothing")
79	        return 1
80	    print(f"red_check: {red} red for the right reason", file=sys.stderr)
81	    return 0
82	
83	
84	if __name__ == "__main__":
85	    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))

"""M1-T14 intent: unusable rows dropped, duplicates resolved, sorted (DATA-02, TC-01)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import RawRow, validate_rows


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_tc01_unusable_rows_and_duplicates(golden):
    given, expected = golden("mdata", "TC-01")
    rows = [RawRow(*r) for r in given["rows"]]
    assert validate_rows(rows, given["tickers"]) == [RawRow(*r) for r in expected["rows"]]


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_requested_ticker_absent_is_a_key_error_naming_it(contract_sample):
    rows = [RawRow(**r) for r in contract_sample("mdata.RawRow")["rows"]]
    with pytest.raises(KeyError, match="ZZZ"):
        validate_rows(rows, ["AAA", "ZZZ"])

def test_requested_ticker_with_only_unusable_rows_is_a_key_error_naming_it(contract_sample):

D216Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/validate.py (25 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/validate.py
"""PL-01 step 2: keep the usable rows, sorted."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Sequence

from mdata.types import RawRow


def validate_rows(rows: Sequence[RawRow], tickers: Sequence[str] | None = None) -> list[RawRow]:
    """Positive closes only, one row per (date, ticker), sorted by (date, ticker).

    Of rows repeating a (date, ticker), the later one in file order wins. A requested ticker
    with no usable row is a KeyError naming it.
    """
    latest = {
        (r.date, r.ticker): r
        for r in rows
        if r.close is not None and r.close > 0 and (tickers is None or r.ticker in tickers)
    }
    found = {ticker for _, ticker in latest}
    for ticker in tickers or ():
        if ticker not in found:
            raise KeyError(ticker)
    return [latest[key] for key in sorted(latest)]

"""validate_rows: positive closes, the later duplicate, sorted by (date, ticker)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import RawRow, validate_rows


def test_sample_keeps_rows_with_a_close_sorted(contract_sample):
    rows = [RawRow(**r) for r in contract_sample("mdata.RawRow")["rows"]]
    kept = validate_rows(rows)
    assert len(kept) == 7
    assert RawRow("2024-03-05", "BBB", None) not in kept
    assert kept == sorted(kept, key=lambda r: (r.date, r.ticker))


def test_sorts_by_date_then_ticker():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 2.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 3.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0),
    ]
    assert validate_rows(rows) == [
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 3.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 2.0),
    ]


def test_keeps_only_requested_tickers():
    rows = [RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0), RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 3.0)]
    assert validate_rows(rows, ["BBB"]) == [RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 3.0)]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("close", [None, 0.0, -2.5])
def test_drops_unusable_closes(close):
    rows = [RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", close), RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 1.0)]
    assert validate_rows(rows) == [RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 1.0)]


def test_later_duplicate_wins_even_after_an_unusable_one():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 2.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", None),
    ]
    assert validate_rows(rows) == [RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 2.0)]


def test_unrequested_ticker_without_rows_is_fine():
    rows = [RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0), RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", None)]
    assert validate_rows(rows) == [RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0)]


def test_missing_requested_ticker_is_a_key_error():
    with pytest.raises(KeyError, match="BBB"):
        validate_rows([RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0)], ["BBB"])

"""M1-T15 intent: one calendar, gaps forward-filled, a long gap an error (DATA-03, DATA-04)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import GapError, RawRow, align_calendar


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_tc02_one_calendar_with_filled_gaps(golden):
    given, expected = golden("mdata", "TC-02")
    prices = align_calendar([RawRow(*r) for r in given["rows"]], max_gap=given["max_gap"])
    assert list(prices.dates) == expected["dates"]
    assert list(prices.tickers) == expected["tickers"]
    assert {t: list(c) for t, c in prices.closes.items()} == expected["closes"]


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_tc03_a_gap_over_max_gap_is_a_gap_error_naming_the_ticker(golden):
    given, expected = golden("mdata", "TC-03")
    assert expected["raises"] == "GapError"
    with pytest.raises(GapError, match=expected["match"]):
        align_calendar([RawRow(*r) for r in given["rows"]], max_gap=given["max_gap"])


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_a_gap_of_exactly_max_gap_is_filled_with_the_previous_close(golden):
    given, _ = golden("mdata", "TC-03")
    rows = [RawRow(*r) for r in given["rows"]]
    prices = align_calendar(rows, max_gap=given["max_gap"] + 1)
    b_rows = {r.date: r.close for r in rows if r.ticker == "B"}
    previous = None
    for day, close in zip(prices.dates, prices.closes["B"], strict=True):
        previous = b_rows.get(day, previous)
        assert close == previous
    assert len(prices.dates) == len({r.date for r in rows})

D231Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py (45 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py
"""PL-01 step 3: put every ticker on one calendar."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence

from mdata.types import GapError, Prices, RawRow


def align_calendar(rows: Sequence[RawRow], *, max_gap: int = 5) -> Prices:
    """One calendar from the first date every ticker has a close; gaps take the previous close.

    The calendar is every date any ticker has a close, from the first date on which every ticker
    has one. More than `max_gap` calendar dates in a row missing for a ticker is a GapError.
    """
    closes: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
    for r in rows:
        if r.close is not None:
            closes.setdefault(r.ticker, {})[r.date] = r.close
    tickers = sorted(closes)
    start = max((min(c) for c in closes.values()), default="")
    dates = sorted({d for c in closes.values() for d in c if d >= start})
    return Prices(
        dates=tuple(dates),
        tickers=tuple(tickers),
        closes={t: _filled(t, closes[t], dates, max_gap) for t in tickers},
    )


def _filled(
    ticker: str, closes: Mapping[str, float], dates: Sequence[str], max_gap: int
) -> tuple[float, ...]:
    """The ticker's close on each date, a missing one taking the previous close."""
    filled: list[float] = []
    missing = 0
    for day in dates:
        if day in closes:
            filled.append(closes[day])
            missing = 0
            continue
        missing += 1
        if missing > max_gap:
            raise GapError(f"{ticker}: more than {max_gap} dates in a row missing, to {day}")
        filled.append(filled[-1])
    return tuple(filled)

  "file_path": "/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/mdata/src/mdata/calendar.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"PL-01 step 3: put every ticker on one calendar.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence\n\nfrom mdata.types import GapError, Prices, RawRow\n\n\ndef align_calendar(rows: Sequence[RawRow], *, max_gap: int = 5) -> Prices:\n    \"\"\"One calendar from the first date every ticker has a close; gaps take the previous close.\n\n    The calendar is every date any ticker has a close, from the first date on which every ticker\n    has one. More than `max_gap` calendar dates in a row missing for a ticker is a GapError.\n    \"\"\"\n    closes: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}\n    for r in rows:\n        if r.close is not None:\n            closes.setdefault(r.ticker, {})[r.date] = r.close\n    tickers = sorted(closes)\n    start = max((min(c) for c in closes.values()), default=\"\")\n    dates = sorted({d for c in closes.values() for d in c if d >= start})\n    return Prices(\n        dates=tuple(dates),\n        tickers=tuple(tickers),\n        closes={t: _filled(t, closes[t], dates, max_gap) for t in tickers},\n    )\n\n\ndef _filled(\n    ticker: str, closes: Mapping[str, float], dates: Sequence[str], max_gap: int\n) -> tuple[float, ...]:\n    \"\"\"The ticker's close on each date, a missing one taking the previous close.\"\"\"\n    filled: list[float] = []\n    missing = 0\n    for day in dates:\n        if day in closes:\n            filled.append(closes[day])\n            missing = 0\n            continue\n        missing += 1\n        if missing > max_gap:\n            raise GapError(f\"{ticker}: more than {max_gap} dates in a row missing, to {day}\")\n        filled.append(filled[-1])\n    return tuple(filled)\n"
}

"""align_calendar: one calendar from the first common date, gaps forward-filled up to max_gap."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import GapError, Prices, RawRow, align_calendar


def test_sample_fills_the_blank_close_with_the_previous_one(contract_sample):
    rows = [RawRow(**r) for r in contract_sample("mdata.RawRow")["rows"]]
    prices = align_calendar(rows)
    assert prices.tickers == ("AAA", "BBB")
    assert prices.dates == ("2024-03-04", "2024-03-05", "2024-03-06", "2024-03-07")
    assert prices.closes == {
        "AAA": (101.5, 102.25, 101.0, 103.4),
        "BBB": (48.2, 48.2, 48.9, 49.3),
    }


def test_dates_ascending_tickers_sorted():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "BBB", 2.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 4.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 1.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 3.0),
    ]
    assert align_calendar(rows) == Prices(
        dates=("2024-03-04", "2024-03-05"),
        tickers=("AAA", "BBB"),
        closes={"AAA": (3.0, 4.0), "BBB": (1.0, 2.0)},
    )


def test_starts_on_the_first_date_every_ticker_has():
    rows = [
        RawRow("2024-03-04", "AAA", 1.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "AAA", 2.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-05", "BBB", 5.0),
        RawRow("2024-03-06", "AAA", 3.0),
    ]
    prices = align_calendar(rows)
    assert prices.dates == ("2024-03-05", "2024-03-06")
    assert prices.closes == {"AAA": (2.0, 3.0), "BBB": (5.0, 5.0)}


def test_trailing_gap_over_max_gap_is_a_gap_error():
    rows = [RawRow("2024-03-04", "BBB", 5.0)]
    rows += [RawRow(f"2024-03-{d:02d}", "AAA", 1.0) for d in range(4, 8)]
    assert align_calendar(rows, max_gap=3).closes["BBB"] == (5.0,) * 4
    with pytest.raises(GapError, match="BBB"):
        align_calendar(rows, max_gap=2)


def test_no_rows_is_an_empty_calendar():
    assert align_calendar([]) == Prices(dates=(), tickers=(), closes={})

<!-- docs/spec.md:25 -->
- **RISK-01** Daily returns are log returns.

D240Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_returns.py (38 lines)

"""M1-T16 intent: daily returns are log returns, dated t (RISK-01, TC-04)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import Prices
from risk import log_returns


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_tc04_geometric_closes(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-04")
    p = given["prices"]
    prices = Prices(
        dates=tuple(p["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(p["tickers"]),
        closes={t: tuple(c) for t, c in p["closes"].items()},
    )
    returns = log_returns(prices)
    assert returns.base_date == expected["base_date"]
    assert list(returns.dates) == expected["dates"]
    assert list(returns.tickers) == expected["tickers"]
    assert set(returns.values) == set(expected["values"])
    for ticker, values in expected["values"].items():
        assert list(returns.values[ticker]) == pytest.approx(values, abs=expected["tol"])


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_fewer_than_two_dates_is_a_value_error(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("mdata.Prices")
    one_day = Prices(
        dates=tuple(s["dates"][:1]),
        tickers=tuple(s["tickers"]),
        closes={t: tuple(c[:1]) for t, c in s["closes"].items()},
    )
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="two dates"):
        log_returns(one_day)

"""PL-02 step 2: daily returns from closes."""

from __future__ import annotations

from itertools import pairwise
from math import log

from mdata.types import Prices
from risk.types import Returns


def log_returns(prices: Prices) -> Returns:
    """r_t = ln(close_t / close_t-1), dated t."""
    if len(prices.dates) < 2:
        raise ValueError(f"log returns need at least two dates, got {len(prices.dates)}")
    return Returns(
        base_date=prices.dates[0],
        dates=prices.dates[1:],
        tickers=prices.tickers,
        values={
            t: tuple(log(c / p) for p, c in pairwise(closes)) for t, closes in prices.closes.items()
        },
    )

"""log_returns: ln(close_t / close_t-1), one per date after the first, per ticker."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from mdata import Prices
from risk import log_returns


def prices_from(s: dict) -> Prices:
    return Prices(
        dates=tuple(s["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(s["tickers"]),
        closes={t: tuple(c) for t, c in s["closes"].items()},
    )


def test_sample_dates_after_the_base_date(contract_sample):
    prices = prices_from(contract_sample("mdata.Prices"))
    returns = log_returns(prices)
    assert returns.base_date == "2024-03-04"
    assert returns.dates == prices.dates[1:]
    assert returns.tickers == ("AAA", "BBB")
    assert {t: len(v) for t, v in returns.values.items()} == {"AAA": 5, "BBB": 5}


def test_doubling_then_halving_is_plus_and_minus_ln2():
    prices = Prices(
        dates=("2024-03-04", "2024-03-05", "2024-03-06"),
        tickers=("AAA", "BBB"),
        closes={"AAA": (10.0, 20.0, 10.0), "BBB": (5.0, 5.0, 5.0)},
    )
    values = log_returns(prices).values
    assert values["AAA"] == pytest.approx((0.6931471805599453, -0.6931471805599453), abs=1e-15)
    assert values["BBB"] == (0.0, 0.0)


def test_returns_sum_to_the_log_of_the_whole_move(contract_sample):
    prices = prices_from(contract_sample("mdata.Prices"))
    values = log_returns(prices).values["AAA"]
    assert sum(values) == pytest.approx(-0.012315170376776375 + 0.0, abs=1e-12)


def test_one_date_is_a_value_error():
    prices = Prices(dates=("2024-03-04",), tickers=("AAA",), closes={"AAA": (1.0,)})
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="at least two dates"):
        log_returns(prices)

<!-- docs/packages/risk/tests.md:16 -->
### TC-05: Rolling volatility from earlier returns
**Pipeline:** PL-02 · **Covers:** RISK-02 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/risk/TC-05/
**Source:** eight hand-made returns, window 4; sample standard deviation of the four returns before each date
**Expected:** no value for the first four dates, then the listed values · **Tolerance:** 1e-12 absolute

<!-- docs/spec.md:27 -->
- **RISK-02** Rolling volatility uses only earlier returns.
  - Given a window of w, when the volatility for date t is computed, then it is the sample standard deviation (n − 1) of the w returns before t, never t's own return, and there is no value until w earlier returns exist.

### Reference RF-01: references/vol_notebook.py
> Reference, not part of the project. Reuse what fits the contract and step table as is, adapt it, or ignore it: your call.
> Never import from references/. Anything you bring over meets the same bar as the rest of the code.
> Caveats: pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy
```python
"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")


def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)


def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
    return vol * np.sqrt(365)


def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})


def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
```

D257Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_vol.py (41 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_vol.py
"""M1-T17 intent: rolling volatility from the w earlier returns only (RISK-02, TC-05)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, rolling_vol


def returns_from(r: dict) -> Returns:
    return Returns(
        base_date=r["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(r["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(r["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in r["values"].items()},
    )


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_tc05_rolling_volatility_from_earlier_returns(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-05")
    returns = returns_from(given["returns"])
    vol = rolling_vol(returns, given["window"])
    assert vol.window == expected["window"]
    assert vol.annualized == expected["annualized"]
    assert vol.returns == returns
    assert set(vol.values) == set(expected["values"])
    for ticker, want in expected["values"].items():
        got = vol.values[ticker]
        assert [v is None for v in got] == [w is None for w in want]
        assert [v for v in got if v is not None] == pytest.approx(
            [w for w in want if w is not None], abs=expected["tol"]
        )


@pytest.mark.intent
@pytest.mark.parametrize("window", [1, 0, -3])
def test_window_below_two_is_a_value_error(contract_sample, window):
    returns = returns_from(contract_sample("risk.Returns"))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="window"):
        rolling_vol(returns, window)

"""PL-02 step 3: rolling volatility from earlier returns."""

from __future__ import annotations

from statistics import stdev

from risk.types import Returns, VolSeries


def rolling_vol(returns: Returns, window: int) -> VolSeries:
    """Sample stdev of the `window` returns before each date, never the date's own."""
    if window < 2:
        raise ValueError(f"window must be at least 2, got {window}")
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=window,
        annualized=False,
        values={
            t: tuple(None if i < window else stdev(v[i - window : i]) for i in range(len(v)))
            for t, v in returns.values.items()
        },
    )

"""rolling_vol: sample stdev of the `window` earlier returns, None until `window` of them exist."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, rolling_vol


def returns_of(values: dict[str, tuple[float, ...]]) -> Returns:
    n = len(next(iter(values.values())))
    dates = tuple(f"2024-03-{d:02d}" for d in range(5, 5 + n))
    return Returns("2024-03-04", dates, tuple(sorted(values)), values)


def test_sample_is_aligned_and_daily(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("risk.Returns")
    returns = Returns(
        s["base_date"],
        tuple(s["dates"]),
        tuple(s["tickers"]),
        {t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()},
    )
    vol = rolling_vol(returns, 2)
    assert vol.returns is returns
    assert vol.window == 2
    assert not vol.annualized
    for values in vol.values.values():
        assert len(values) == 5
        assert values[:2] == (None, None)
        assert all(v is not None and v > 0 for v in values[2:])


def test_uses_earlier_returns_never_the_dates_own():
    # The third return is huge: only the date after it may see it.
    vol = rolling_vol(returns_of({"AAA": (0.01, 0.03, 9.0, 0.0)}), 2)
    assert vol.values["AAA"][2] == pytest.approx(0.01414213562373095)
    assert vol.values["AAA"][3] > 1


def test_window_slides_old_returns_out():
    # Two early outliers, then flat: once both leave the window, the volatility is 0.
    vol = rolling_vol(returns_of({"AAA": (5.0, -5.0, 0.01, 0.01, 0.01, 0.01)}), 2)
    assert vol.values["AAA"][2] > 1
    assert vol.values["AAA"][4:] == (0.0, 0.0)


def test_sample_stdev_divides_by_n_minus_one():
    # Returns 0 and 0.02: population stdev 0.01, sample stdev 0.01 * sqrt(2).
    vol = rolling_vol(returns_of({"AAA": (0.0, 0.02, 0.0)}), 2)
    assert vol.values["AAA"][2] == pytest.approx(0.01414213562373095, abs=1e-15)


def test_constant_returns_have_zero_volatility():
    vol = rolling_vol(returns_of({"AAA": (0.01,) * 5}), 3)
    assert vol.values["AAA"] == (None, None, None, 0.0, 0.0)


def test_window_of_one_is_a_value_error():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="at least 2"):
        rolling_vol(returns_of({"AAA": (0.01, 0.02, 0.03)}), 1)

### Reference RF-01: references/vol_notebook.py
> Reference, not part of the project. Reuse what fits the contract and step table as is, adapt it, or ignore it: your call.
> Never import from references/. Anything you bring over meets the same bar as the rest of the code.
> Caveats: pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy
```python
"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")


def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)


def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
    return vol * np.sqrt(365)


def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})


def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
```

D272Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/tests/intent/risk/test_annualize.py (48 lines)

"""M1-T18 intent: daily volatility times sqrt(252), gaps kept; never twice (RISK-03, TC-06)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, annualize


def vol_from(s: dict) -> VolSeries:
    r = s["returns"]
    returns = Returns(
        base_date=r["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(r["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(r["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in r["values"].items()},
    )
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=s["window"],
        annualized=s["annualized"],
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()},
    )


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_tc06_annualizing(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-06")
    daily = vol_from(given["vol"])
    vol = annualize(daily)
    assert vol.annualized == expected["annualized"]
    assert vol.returns == daily.returns
    assert vol.window == daily.window
    assert set(vol.values) == set(expected["values"])
    for ticker, want in expected["values"].items():
        got = vol.values[ticker]
        assert [v is None for v in got] == [w is None for w in want]
        assert [v for v in got if v is not None] == pytest.approx(
            [w for w in want if w is not None], abs=expected["tol"]
        )


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_annualizing_an_annualized_series_is_a_value_error(contract_sample):
    vol = vol_from(contract_sample("risk.VolSeries"))
    assert vol.annualized
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="annualized"):
        annualize(vol)

"""PL-02 step 4: annualize daily volatility over trading days."""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import replace
from math import sqrt

from risk.types import VolSeries


def annualize(vol: VolSeries, periods_per_year: int = 252) -> VolSeries:
    """Each daily value times sqrt(periods_per_year); gaps stay None."""
    if vol.annualized:
        raise ValueError("vol is already annualized")
    scale = sqrt(periods_per_year)
    return replace(
        vol,
        annualized=True,
        values={
            t: tuple(None if v is None else v * scale for v in values)
            for t, values in vol.values.items()
        },
    )

"""annualize: each daily value times sqrt(periods_per_year), gaps kept, never twice."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, annualize

RETURNS = Returns(
    "2024-03-04",
    ("2024-03-05", "2024-03-06", "2024-03-07"),
    ("AAA",),
    {"AAA": (0.01, -0.02, 0.005)},
)


def test_daily_series_comes_back_annualized_on_the_same_returns():
    daily = VolSeries(RETURNS, 2, False, {"AAA": (None, None, 0.02)})
    vol = annualize(daily)
    assert vol.annualized
    assert vol.returns is RETURNS
    assert vol.window == 2
    assert vol.values["AAA"][:2] == (None, None)
    assert vol.values["AAA"][2] is not None


def test_scales_by_root_of_periods_per_year():
    daily = VolSeries(RETURNS, 2, False, {"AAA": (None, None, 0.02)})
    assert annualize(daily).values["AAA"][2] == pytest.approx(0.02 * 252**0.5, abs=1e-15)
    assert annualize(daily, periods_per_year=4).values["AAA"][2] == pytest.approx(0.04)


def test_input_left_daily():
    daily = VolSeries(RETURNS, 2, False, {"AAA": (None, None, 0.02)})
    annualize(daily)
    assert not daily.annualized
    assert daily.values["AAA"][2] == 0.02


def test_annualized_input_is_a_value_error():
    vol = VolSeries(RETURNS, 2, True, {"AAA": (None, None, 0.3)})
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="already annualized"):
        annualize(vol)

<!-- docs/spec.md:31 -->
- **RISK-04** Each ticker gets a whole-period summary.
  - Given returns, when summarized, then the annualized return is 252 × the mean, the annualized volatility is √252 × the sample standard deviation, the Sharpe ratio is their quotient (0 when the volatility is 0; no risk-free rate), and the last rolling volatility is reported.

### Reference RF-01: references/vol_notebook.py
> Reference, not part of the project. Reuse what fits the contract and step table as is, adapt it, or ignore it: your call.
> Never import from references/. Anything you bring over meets the same bar as the rest of the code.
> Caveats: pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy
```python
"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")


def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)


def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
    return vol * np.sqrt(365)


def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})


def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
```

"""M1-T19 intent: annualized return, volatility, Sharpe and last rolling vol (RISK-04, TC-07)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, summary_stats


def vol_from(s: dict) -> VolSeries:
    r = s["returns"]
    returns = Returns(
        base_date=r["base_date"],
        dates=tuple(r["dates"]),
        tickers=tuple(r["tickers"]),
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in r["values"].items()},
    )
    return VolSeries(
        returns=returns,
        window=s["window"],
        annualized=s["annualized"],
        values={t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()},
    )


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_tc07_whole_period_summary(golden):
    given, expected = golden("risk", "TC-07")
    summary = summary_stats(vol_from(given["vol"]))
    tol = expected["tol"]
    assert [s.ticker for s in summary] == [e["ticker"] for e in expected["summary"]]
    for got, want in zip(summary, expected["summary"], strict=True):
        assert got.ann_return == pytest.approx(want["ann_return"], abs=tol)
        assert got.ann_vol == pytest.approx(want["ann_vol"], abs=tol)
        assert got.sharpe == pytest.approx(want["sharpe"], abs=tol)
        assert got.last_vol == pytest.approx(want["last_vol"], abs=tol)


@pytest.mark.intent
def test_fewer_than_two_returns_is_a_value_error(contract_sample):
    s = contract_sample("risk.VolSeries")
    s["returns"]["dates"] = s["returns"]["dates"][:1]
    s["returns"]["values"] = {t: v[:1] for t, v in s["returns"]["values"].items()}
    s["values"] = {t: v[:1] for t, v in s["values"].items()}
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="two returns"):
        summary_stats(vol_from(s))

D291Write/Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/summary.py (34 lines)
wrote /Users/connorott/plugins/dev-team-workspace/iteration-b1/minilab-A0-r2/with_skill/project/packages/risk/src/risk/summary.py
"""PL-03 step 2: each ticker's whole-period summary."""

from __future__ import annotations

from math import sqrt
from statistics import mean, stdev

from risk.types import TickerSummary, VolSeries


def summary_stats(vol: VolSeries, periods_per_year: int = 252) -> tuple[TickerSummary, ...]:
    """Annualized return, volatility and Sharpe per ticker, with its last rolling volatility.

    The return is periods_per_year times the mean daily return, the volatility sqrt(periods_per_year)
    times their sample stdev; Sharpe is their quotient, 0 when the volatility is 0. The last
    rolling volatility is the latest date's value that exists, None if none does.
    """
    if len(vol.returns.dates) < 2:
        raise ValueError(f"a summary needs at least two returns, got {len(vol.returns.dates)}")
    summaries = []
    for ticker in vol.returns.tickers:
        returns = vol.returns.values[ticker]
        ann_return = periods_per_year * mean(returns)
        ann_vol = sqrt(periods_per_year) * stdev(returns)
        summaries.append(
            TickerSummary(
                ticker=ticker,
                ann_return=ann_return,
                ann_vol=ann_vol,
                sharpe=ann_return / ann_vol if ann_vol else 0.0,
                last_vol=next((v for v in reversed(vol.values[ticker]) if v is not None), None),
            )
        )
    return tuple(summaries)

"""summary_stats: one annualized summary per ticker, in ticker order, with its last rolling vol."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from risk import Returns, VolSeries, summary_stats

DATES = ("2024-03-05", "2024-03-06", "2024-03-07", "2024-03-08")


def vol_from(s: dict) -> VolSeries:
    r = s["returns"]
    returns = Returns(
        r["base_date"],
        tuple(r["dates"]),
        tuple(r["tickers"]),
        {t: tuple(v) for t, v in r["values"].items()},
    )
    return VolSeries(
        returns, s["window"], s["annualized"], {t: tuple(v) for t, v in s["values"].items()}
    )


def vol_of(returns: tuple[float, ...], values: tuple[float | None, ...]) -> VolSeries:
    r = Returns("2024-03-04", DATES[: len(returns)], ("AAA",), {"AAA": returns})
    return VolSeries(r, 2, True, {"AAA": values})


def test_sample_matches_the_ticker_summary_sample(contract_sample):
    summary = summary_stats(vol_from(contract_sample("risk.VolSeries")))
    assert [x.ticker for x in summary] == ["AAA", "BBB"]
    want = contract_sample("risk.TickerSummary")
    assert summary[0].ann_return == pytest.approx(want["ann_return"], abs=1e-12)
    assert summary[0].ann_vol == pytest.approx(want["ann_vol"], abs=1e-12)
    assert summary[0].sharpe == pytest.approx(want["sharpe"], abs=1e-12)
    assert summary[0].last_vol == want["last_vol"]


def test_annualizes_over_periods_per_year():
    # Returns 0.01 and 0.03: mean 0.02, sample stdev sqrt(0.0002).
    (x,) = summary_stats(vol_of((0.01, 0.03), (None, None)), periods_per_year=4)
    assert x.ann_return == pytest.approx(0.08, abs=1e-15)
    assert x.ann_vol == pytest.approx(2 * 0.0002**0.5, abs=1e-15)
    assert x.sharpe == pytest.approx(0.08 / (2 * 0.0002**0.5), abs=1e-12)


def test_zero_volatility_has_zero_sharpe():
    (x,) = summary_stats(vol_of((0.002, 0.002, 0.002), (None, None, 0.0)))
    assert x.ann_vol == 0
    assert x.sharpe == 0
    assert x.ann_return == pytest.approx(0.504, abs=1e-12)


def test_last_vol_is_the_latest_value_that_exists():
    (x,) = summary_stats(vol_of((0.01, 0.02, -0.01, 0.0), (None, None, 0.2, None)))
    assert x.last_vol == 0.2
    (y,) = summary_stats(vol_of((0.01, 0.02), (None, None)))
    assert y.last_vol is None


def test_one_return_is_a_value_error():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="at least two returns"):
        summary_stats(vol_of((0.01,), (None,)))

`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Returns: base_date: str; dates: tuple[str, ...]; tickers: tuple[str, ...]; values: Mapping[str, tuple[float, ...]]`
- **Invariants:** `dates` are the prices' dates after the first, which is `base_date`; one value per date and ticker
- **Used by:** PL-02, PL-03

<!-- docs/packages/risk/contract.md:24 -->
### risk.Drawdown: Worst fall from a peak
**Kind:** type · **Sample:** `tests/contracts/risk.Drawdown/sample.json` · **Cases:** one ticker's fall from its peak
`@dataclass(frozen=True) class Drawdown: ticker: str; depth: float; peak_date: str`
- **Invariants:** `depth` ≤ 0
- **Used by:** PL-03

<!-- docs/packages/risk/tests.md:31 -->
### TC-08: Drawdown with dates
**Pipeline:** PL-03 · **Covers:** RISK-05 · **Kind:** synthetic known-answer · **Fixture:** tests/fixtures/risk/TC-08/
**Source:** closes 100, 110, 99, 88, 95, 120 (X), a rising series (Y), a first-day fall (Z)
**Expected:** X −20% from the second date to the fourth; Y 0 at the first date; Z peaks at the first date · **Tolerance:** 1e-12 absolute on depth, dates exact

<!-- docs/spec.md:33 -->
- **RISK-05** Maximum drawdown comes with its dates.
  - Given closes, when the drawdown is computed, then wealth starts at 1 on the first date, and the result is the deepest fall below the running peak (trough / peak − 1, at most 0) with the date of that peak and the date of the trough; the first if tied. With no fall, both dates are the first date.

### RF-01: Volatility notebook
**File:** `references/vol_notebook.py` · **Hash:** 1e1d95d0 · **Kind:** code · **Milestones:** M1
- **Purpose:** the user's pandas notebook for returns, rolling volatility, annualizing, a summary and drawdown
- **Key parts:** `daily_returns`, `rolling_vol(returns, window)`, `annualize`, `summary`, `max_drawdown`
- **In → Out:** dates × tickers closes → returns, rolling vol, a summary frame, drawdown per ticker
- **Use for:** PL-02 `rolling_vol` and `annualize`, PL-03 `summary_stats` and `max_drawdown` design
- **Caveats:** pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy

### Reference RF-01: references/vol_notebook.py
> Reference, not part of the project. Reuse what fits the contract and step table as is, adapt it, or ignore it: your call.
> Never import from references/. Anything you bring over meets the same bar as the rest of the code.
> Caveats: pandas and numpy, which the project doesn't use (AD-01): reimplement, don't copy
```python
"""My old notebook code for volatility and drawdown (pandas). Kept for the formulas, not to import."""
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


def daily_returns(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Log returns of a dates x tickers close panel."""
    return np.log(prices / prices.shift(1)).dropna(how="all")


def rolling_vol(returns: pd.DataFrame, window: int = 20) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Rolling volatility over the last `window` days, today included."""
    return returns.rolling(window).apply(lambda x: np.std(x), raw=True)


def annualize(vol: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Annualize to a calendar year, like my brokerage statements: 365 days."""
    return vol * np.sqrt(365)


def summary(returns: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Yearly return and volatility per ticker."""
    return pd.DataFrame({"ann_return": returns.mean() * 365, "ann_vol": returns.std(ddof=0) * np.sqrt(365)})


def max_drawdown(prices: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.Series:
    """Deepest fall below the running high."""
    return (prices / prices.cummax() - 1).min()
```

full text
The build is stopped at M1-T20 and needs your decision on a contract change. M1-T01 through M1-T19 are done on `build/M1` (19 of 21 tasks); M1-T20 and M1-T21 are still open, so I haven't asked about merging yet.

**Where things stand**
- Each task's gate passed before its tick and commit. Each split task (M1-T14 to M1-T19) has a `<task>: tests` commit, checked red for the right reason, before its code commit.
- PL-01, PL-02 and PL-03 all run end to end in their smoke tests. Golden cases TC-01 to TC-07 pass.
- I haven't run the checkpoint reviews or `scripts/check.sh full` yet.

**Why M1-T20 can't go ahead**
The approved contract freezes `risk.Drawdown` as `ticker, depth, peak_date`. But RISK-05 asks for the trough date as well, and TC-08's `expected.json` checks a `trough_date` for each ticker. So no test can check M1-T20's "both dates exact" against what's frozen. I froze the contract as written at M1-T01, and CR-09 doesn't let a later task add the field.

The same gap reaches the report: REP-01 asks for "both dates", but the `write_report` I built in M1-T10 can only write `peak_date`. `dt escalate` refused to record this ("no claim for this worktree"), because there are no worktree claims in this single-session build. So this report is the escalation.

**What I'd suggest**
1. Revise the contract with `architecture-design`: add `trough_date: str` to `risk.Drawdown` in `docs/packages/risk/contract.md`.
2. Replan: add a re-freeze interface task. It would update `risk/types.py`, the `risk.Drawdown` and `risk.Report` samples and the contract test, and add `trough_date` to the report row in `risk/report.py`. Unless you'd rather M1-T20 take on the `report.py` change.
3. I then commit the re-freeze, move `checks-base`, run its gate, and carry on with M1-T20 and M1-T21 in the re-read plan's order.

Do you want to go ahead with that revision? Or would you prefer to drop the trough date from RISK-05/TC-08 instead? That would mean changing an oracle file, which needs your approval under CR-05.

**One deviation from your steps**
After M1-T11, M1-T12 and M1-T13, I ran `dt prebuild-clear PL-0N`, which `dt land` would normally run, and included the `docs/packages/*/pipelines.md` changes in those task commits. Those docs aren't in the tasks' `Writes:`.
